# Changelog

All notable changes to Fenolite are documented here. The format follows
[Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/) and the project uses
[Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/) (pre-1.0: minor versions may break).

## [Unreleased]

- c0462 tasks 5.2 and 5.3: the pour and conversion censuses pass as pinned on `cac/lote-29`. No polygon is refused for a rule scope or a connect scope. A polygon is refused `rule-unmapped` only for a record that the design names (`IGNOREPADTOPADCLEARANCEINFOOTPRINT=TRUE`, an object matrix refused `matrix-kind`), which `test_altium_rule_kinds_c0462.py` now checks per row; the scenario and task 5.2 say so (coordinator, 2026-11-06).
- The KiCad rules writer writes the selector leaves `zone`, `hole` and `pad_type` for KiCad 9 too (c0462 task 3.2): their condition probes are recorded `present` on 9.0.9 (the pinned image), as on 10.0.6, so a rule that uses one is no longer a loss on 9. `H-K-DRU-LEAF-C0462` is `KICAD-VERIFIED (9.0.x, 10.0.x)`.
- The Altium writer's evidence no longer names `H-A-RULE-SHORT` and `H-A-RULE-ANTENNA` (c0465 task 6.2): both rows are `UNVERIFIED` until the kit of task 6.1, and a declared `INFERRED` may not name a weaker row. Task 6.2's checks pass on the lote-29 tip.
- The Altium writer writes an empty board text and a text that spans lines (c0460 task 2.5, `H-A-TEXT-CRLF`): a line feed is written as CR LF, as Altium stores it. Public row `-08` held 51 empty union-member texts and 13 texts with CR LF that the conversion left out; the census ledgers lose their empty-text and line-break rows.
- Altium region members keep their union through flow A (c0460 task 2.4): `import --to python` maps board graphics to the uuids the build gives them, as it does copper and texts; public row `-01`'s union came back empty from flow A.
- Altium tracks and arcs keep their union through KiCad and flow A (c0460 task 2.4, S-2190). Public row `-07`'s round trip lost all 47 members of its 5 length-tuning unions: the import read a union index on vias, regions and components only. It now reads a track's index at 36 and an arc's at 48 of their long records, and the Altium writer writes a member track or arc in that long form. `import --to python` also names the members of `Fenolite.Altium.UnionMembers` by the uuids the build gives table rows; flow A lost row `-04`'s 22 via members the same way. Free texts keep their union too (index at 119), and a member component's designator and comment take the component's: public row `-08` comes back with 2 671 of its 2 724 members (Altium read 434 at 332b79837); its 51 empty texts come back with task 2.5. Footprint tracks of a union are not carried.
- Altium stroke texts keep the font their record names through KiCad (c0460 task 1.4, S-2253). The import keeps a stroke text's font name, bold and italic in its `altium` bag (`stroke_font`), a conversion to KiCad carries them by KiCad uuid as `Fenolite.Altium.StrokeFonts`, and the Altium writer writes them back; the round trip of public row `-04` wrote an empty `FONTNAME` on its 15 stroke comments (S-2191). Public rows `-01` to `-07` now return the font of every stroke text.
- Altium signal layer names survive a trip through KiCad (c0466 task 5.1, S-2254 to S-2256). The round trip of public row `-04` wrote its inner layers `Internal Layer 1/2` as `Mid-Layer 1/2`; rows `-01` and `-07` lost their layer names the same way. A converted copper row now takes its Altium name as KiCad user name when the writer would write another, and the Altium writer names a signal layer after its KiCad row's user name (a KiCad board's own names included), unless a record cannot hold it, it names another Altium layer or two layers take it. KiCad names such a row's Gerber file after its user name. `PcbDocSpec.plane_names` is now `copper_names`.
- An Altium board keeps its frame and origin through KiCad (c0467, S-2260 to S-2262). The round trip of public row `-04` moved every Y by +29.528 mil relative to the origin, and every round trip moved the absolute coordinates, because the way back anchored the board at its corner with the origin there. The conversion to KiCad now carries the source's origin and the edge's corner as `Fenolite.Altium.Frame`; the conversion back, and flow A through `import --to python` and `build`, write the board at its coordinates with the origin moved with the board. A board that would lie at a negative coordinate is anchored as before, with one `outline` change.
- Tests: the coordinator approves c0462's extension key `layer_value` (a per-layer value of an Altium rule record) in the model review, and the reader's new rule rows (`H-A-RULE-MATRIX-NPTH` among them) are no claim of the Altium build.
- Docs: the kit request S-2233 (c0464) and the open repeat and OutJob steps of c0389 name Altium Designer 24.5, which the maintainer accepts in place of 26 since 2026-11-06.
- A per-layer Width or Differential Pairs Routing rule (c0462 task 4.1) no longer breaks the import of a board that holds one, nor a conversion back from KiCad (`FEN-1001`, `KeyError: 'layer_value'`, on public row `-01`): its bag key `layer_value` joins the closed table `EXT_KEYS`.
- Altium Short-Circuit and Net Antennae records map onto `short` and `antenna` rules where one enabled record of the document relaxes the check (c0465 tasks 5.1 and 5.2, design D5): `ALLOWED=TRUE` gives severity `ignore`, `FALSE` gives `error`, and `NETANTENNAETOLERANCE` is the `max`. Records that relax nothing stay `checked`. The public rows `-04` and `-07` gain one `antenna` rule of 1 mil each, which their conversion to KiCad now reports as one `rule` loss (KiCad 10.0 checks no antenna rule), where the record was kept for an Altium write before. The warning `altium.rules.check-relaxed` is withdrawn. The Altium writer writes both kinds back with the keys of the public records (S-2243): kind numbers 15 and 56; a `short` of severity `warning` has no record and is reported `value-unsupported`.
- A per-layer Width or Differential Pairs Routing rule (c0462 task 4.1) no longer breaks the import of a board that holds one, nor a conversion back from KiCad (`FEN-1001`, `KeyError: 'layer_value'`, on public row `-01`): its bag key `layer_value` joins the closed table `EXT_KEYS`. The row's per-layer keys name only mid-layers, which its internal planes do not take; the document written back from KiCad declares the two inner layers as mid-layers, so only the way back gave the rules, and only a heavy corpus test converts that far. A unit test now checks every pair key of the rule mapper against the table, and an authored per-layer Width converts to KiCad and back.
- Polygon Connect Style scopes are read with the source's document in the pour and in the conversion to KiCad (c0462 task 4.5): the 5 public records it refused (`HasFootprint` 3, a layer function 1, `IsNamedPolygon` inside a larger query 1) now map, so no public polygon is refused `connect-scope`. Rows `-06` (1) and `-08` (2) now stop at `rule-unmapped`, on a Clearance matrix task 4.3 refuses; the 5 polygons of `-01` now pour and each fails against the saved regions (4 by M1), a known item for c0463. The conversion's zones and pads gain their pour name, pad type and hole as scope subjects; a scope that names a layer or a room stays unjudged there, since the zones carry KiCad layer names.
- Each pad and via converted from Altium takes the mask and paste expansion of the record that governs it (c0462 task 4.4, design D6): the first enabled record, by priority, whose scope, read with the full grammar and the document, selects it by net, the net classes that hold the net, component, layer, hole and pad type. Until now every pad took the record of scope `All`, and a pad under any other record was a `pad-margin` loss. A record the conversion cannot judge for the object (outside the grammar, a room, or a layer of an object that spans the layers when the rest of the scope does not decide it) is still reported. Level 3 of `equivalent` chooses the same record. The public rows hold only `All` and `IsVia` records, so no census moves.
- Altium object matrices of Clearance and Board Outline Clearance map cell by cell (c0462 task 4.3, S-2217, S-2249). A surface pad and a through-hole pad that differ become `pad_type smd` and `or(pad_type th, pad_type npth)`; a free fill or region is a copper `graphic`, text `item_kind text` (a loss on the KiCad target). A Board Outline Clearance matrix pairs object kinds with the outline and a cutout, one `edge_clearance` rule per differing kind. A matrix that tells apart an arc and a track, a fill and a region, or the outline and a cutout is refused `matrix-kind`: no neutral kind tells them apart. A `Hole`, cavity or split-line entry changes no rule. A record whose matrix holds an entry is kept for the Altium writer. On the public rows every matrix is proved cell by cell; rows `-06` (2) and `-08` (1) are refused. The heavy row's refused matrix was refused for its scope before, so the pour census counts its 13 polygons `rule-unmapped` in place of `rule-scope`.
- The public copper census holds the Clearance rules c0462's scope functions read (c0462): row `-04` judges with 2 clearance rules (a record scoped to one footprint maps), row `-07` with 3 (a record of the polygons and a differential pair maps); both stay with one Clearance record unread. `tests/corpus/test_altium_copper.py` failed on both rows since the scope tasks.
- Empty Altium mechanical slots come back as the source left them after a trip through KiCad (c0464 task 6). The `Board6.Settings` carrier also holds the enabled flags of mechanical layers 1 to 16. A slot that nothing of the written document lies on now takes the source's flag, and a slot that no primitive lies on takes the source's name; a body that the writer placed there does not count. Before, the writer enabled Mechanical 13 to 16 always and 1 to 12 only when used, and named a slot after the KiCad layer mapped to it. A drawing on the KiCad row `Altium.DrillDrawing` or `Altium.DrillGuide` now goes back to Altium's drill drawing or drill guide layer instead of Mechanical 8. Public rows `-02`, `-04` and `-06` give every mechanical name and flag back through flow A.
- `import --to python` writes a straight arc as the line it draws (c0427 task 2.6). An Altium arc of a tiny sweep reads as three points rounded to the nanometre that may lie on one line, and `design.arc()` takes no such arc, so the import left it out as a `drawing`: a private sweep counted 1107 user and mechanical arcs on 16 boards. When the middle point lies between the ends, the arc is now `design.line()` from its start to its end, on its layer and of its width. An arc of one point, or whose middle point lies beyond an end, stays a `drawing`.
- Copper graphics on a net whose name holds a slash keep their net through KiCad 10 (c0425). Such a graphic's `(net "…")` slot names the net in the board's stored spelling (`A1{slash}GND`), but the reader names the net `A1/GND`, and `with_graphic_nets` matched the model name only. The graphic therefore read back without a net. Every copy of a netted free region in an Altium embedded board array (nets `B<k>/<net>`) gave a level 5 `copper-no-net`, and the conversion was refused. The importer also declared the stored spelling as a net of its own. `with_graphic_nets` and the importer's `graphic_net` now take either spelling.
- Altium internal planes survive a trip through KiCad (c0466, S-2250 to S-2252). The conversion to KiCad writes each plane as a `power` row named after the plane and carries its net, pull-back and Split Plane polygons in the board property `Fenolite.Altium.Planes`; the conversion back writes the planes again, `(Multiple Nets)` planes included, with their names and splits, and counts a plane that KiCad no longer holds as one lost under `plane`. Rows `-01` and `-02` now come back with Ground Plane and Power Plane (or GND and POWER) instead of two signal mid layers, and the read-back no longer gains a per-layer Width rule for each of them.
- The c0462 scope oracle is recorded (task 5.1, S-2214): `tests/data/altium/kit/scope-oracle-c0462.json` holds the object counts per kind that the PCB filter of Altium Designer 24.5.2.23 selected for the mapped scopes of 7 public rows (row `-01` not measured), and `tests/corpus/test_altium_scope_oracle_c0462.py` compares Fenolite's selectors with them. Three readings change with it. `CompPinCount` compared so that a free object (0 pins) complies now selects every object but the components that do not comply. `InNet('No Net')` now selects no object (`H-A-RD-PRJ-SCOPE-NONET` refuted, `-NONET-2`). `InDifferentialPairClass` now selects the pairs' copper for every rule kind, not only for a rule on pairs. The differences that stay are pinned with their reasons: plane split lines, keep-out arcs, footprint copper, the vias of components, one polygon of row `-02` and `Not IsDifferentialPair`. `record` takes `--not-measured` and `--extra`, and numbers scopes by the plan as run; the comparison shapes arcs at the pour's tolerance, so row `-08` runs in seconds. The pour census of row `-08` refuses no polygon for a rule scope any more: 14 for a connect scope and 19 as `depends-on-refused`.
- The Altium edit session closes five gaps found on the maintainer's board (c0452, S-2110). A free region now moves: its twin in `ShapeBasedRegions6` is found before the region's record changes (the move raised `ValueError`). An item is the nearest record within 0.002 mm of what it states, so coordinates rounded to the micrometre, as `fenolite inspect` shows them, name their record, and `Track` and the new `Arc` take an optional `width` that tells coincident tracks apart. New items `Arc` and `Fill` are moved and deleted, and `move` takes a `Polygon`: its outline and its poured primitives move together. `set_outline` gives a polygon a new outline of straight edges and removes its pour. `create` adds arcs, fills, free regions (both forms) and unpoured polygons, each cloned from a record of its kind that the document holds. `set_property` writes an arc's width at the arc's own offset. The new gates `create` and `set_outline` are off, like every other, and rest on the new rows `H-A-EDIT-CREATE` and `H-A-EDIT-SHAPE`. Phase B of Part J writes `shapes`, `create` and `set_outline`.
- TrueType texts survive a trip through KiCad (c0460 task 1.3). The KiCad reader now projects `face`, `bold` and `italic` of a board text, footprint text or field whose font names a face, so a text read from KiCad holds the font a text read from Altium holds and the conversion back writes it as a TrueType text. Stroke-font texts keep `bold` and `italic` in their slots, and their canonical JSON is unchanged.
- The kit sample `pour-hatched` and the tool of the Repour oracle (c0463 tasks 3.1 and 4.1, commands ready, no run yet). The sample holds a polygon of each hatch style, two that show the hatch grid's origin, one with a slanted edge, a ring that touches itself and one that crosses itself; kit step K6.12 repours it in Altium (hypothesis `H-A-POUR-HATCH`). The Altium writer writes a zone's `hatch_style`, `grid_size` and `track_width` as `HATCHSTYLE`, `GRIDSIZE` and `TRACKWIDTH`. `tools/kit_pour_c0463.py prepare` writes the run's folder (the sample and the eight public rows of the pour census) and `record` reads the boards Altium saved into `tests/data/altium/kit/` records of counts, areas and hashes (`backends.altium.pourrecord`); a row's record copies none of its geometry.
- The computed Altium pour reads row -02 (c0463 task 4.3, S-2227): a Clearance record with the in-footprint pad-pair flag no longer refuses the pour, which pairs no pads; a polygon cut-out keeps no clearance band; a via without a pad (diameter equal to drill) is joined by a polygon of its net. All 12 polygons of row -02 now pour; against Altium 24.5's repour 10 pass and the GND planes fail M1 (4.46 and 3.98) by a band along the board's edge that Altium pours and its one Clearance would keep free, a known difference. On row -06 the pad-pair record now maps (one polygon moves to `depends-on-refused`).
- The computed Altium pour keeps Altium's distance from the board's edge and draws a relief conductor over its pad (c0463 task 4.3, S-2227). Altium 24.5's repour of the public rows keeps the governing edge clearance over cos(π/n) from the board's edge (162.35 µm for a rule of 150 µm, 305.86 µm for 300 µm), n the fewest sides, a multiple of 8, within 0.5 mil of the circle; a document without a Board Outline Clearance record keeps its governing Clearance there (row -03), where the pour kept none. A conductor of a pad that the polygon's outline cuts now runs over the pad outside the outline, as on row -07. Every polygon of rows -04 and -07 that reaches the board's edge now meets M1 against Altium's repour (row -04's planes went from 7.4 times the allowance to 0.45). `tests/corpus/test_altium_pour_hatched_c0463.py` read a field of a difference by a wrong name (`size` for `area`) on a path the passing sample never took; fixed. The neck removal now parts a pinch that the round opening's discs, grown back, close again (I10, S-2227): row -04's `POURINDEX=7` goes from 3.95 times the M1 allowance to 1.99, and row -05's top polygon passes M3 and M4.
- The computed Altium pour pours a hatched polygon (c0463 task 3.2, S-2223, hypothesis `H-A-POUR-HATCH`). Altium 24.5's repour of the kit sample `pour-hatched` shows the model: the solid pour's area with every clearance, air gap and board clearance grown by half the track width, a track along each of its edges, the grid lines at whole multiples of `GRIDSIZE` from the document's origin inside it, and the relief conductors from each pad's centre. The writer writes a hatch as tracks of its polygon (no region), the self-check reads them back, and `pourcompare` strokes the tracks and arcs of a saved hatch. Each of the sample's seven hatches meets M1 to M4 against Altium's (`tests/corpus/test_altium_pour_hatched_c0463.py`). `hatched` now refuses only a `None` or unknown style or a width not below the grid. The S-2223 row of `docs/evidence/sources.md` named the four relief conductors "border tracks"; it is corrected.
- `tests/corpus/test_altium_pour_repour_c0463.py` compares Fenolite's pour with Altium 24.5's Repour All (c0463 task 4.2). With the corpus only, it names the polygons whose saved pour is not Altium's repour; with `FENOLITE_ALTIUM_REPOUR_DIR` naming the folder of a Repour run, it checks the saved boards against the record and compares Fenolite's pour of each by M1 to M4 with the regions Altium poured.
- The records of Altium 24.5's repour are in `tests/data/altium/kit/` (c0463, S-2223, S-2224): `pour-hatched.json` lists the tracks Altium pours for each hatch style of the kit sample, and `repour.json` holds, for each polygon of the eight public rows, the counts, areas and ring hashes of Altium's Repour All, without a vertex list. Rows 03, 05 and 07 had saved exactly that pour; the others differ by at most 0.13% of a polygon's area.
- The kit sample `pour-hatched` and the tool of the Repour oracle (c0463 tasks 3.1 and 4.1). The sample holds a polygon of each hatch style, two that show the hatch grid's origin, one with a slanted edge, a ring that touches itself and one that crosses itself; kit step K6.12 repours it in Altium (hypothesis `H-A-POUR-HATCH`). The Altium writer writes a zone's `hatch_style`, `grid_size` and `track_width` as `HATCHSTYLE`, `GRIDSIZE` and `TRACKWIDTH`. `tools/kit_pour_c0463.py prepare` writes the run's folder (the sample and the eight public rows of the pour census) and `record` reads the boards Altium saved into `tests/data/altium/kit/` records of counts, areas and hashes (`backends.altium.pourrecord`); a row's record copies none of its geometry.
- An Altium polygon whose outline holds arc vertices goes through a conversion and `import --to python` (c0463 task 1.3, c0427 task 2.4). The import gave such a polygon no outline, so the KiCad target flattened it at a fixed 5000 nm, the Python import reported it `zone-outline`, and a rewrite skipped it. The import now gives it the ring the pour builds: each arc chorded within the polygon's `ARCRESOLUTION` (`backends/altium/adapter/arcring.py`, shared with the pour, which no longer imports `math`). The conversion to KiCad counts the zone `changed` and carries the source vertices and `ARCRESOLUTION` in the board property `Fenolite.Altium.PolygonArcs`, keyed by the KiCad zone's uuid (derived from the polygon's `UNIQUEID` when it has one). The way back writes the source's arc vertices while the zone's outline is still their ring, moved by a translation at most, and the chords as a `changed` zone otherwise. `zone-outline` stays for a polygon without a readable `ARCRESOLUTION`, an arc that cannot be placed, and a KiCad zone with fewer than three points.
- The board view's server reads and drops a refused request's body (up to 1 MiB, 5 s) before it answers. On Windows a socket closed with unread data resets the connection, so a client sending an oversized body saw an abort instead of the 413; dev's CI run 42241222141 failed once on it (`test_run_guard_refuses_other_origins_and_starts_nothing`).
- Altium 24.5 opens the round trip of NG6011 (public row `-04`) with its models (c0449, task 4.3, S-2084): after a relaunch, 0 dialogs and 0 messages, 13 STEP bodies, and U1, J1 and D1 show their own model names and the source's heights. The wrong names of the read before came from Altium's in-session model cache, not from the file. A round trip written with the carried model identities (S-2085) shows its own model names even in a session that has just opened the first round trip, whose model `ID`s it does not share.
- An Altium model that comes back from KiCad with its source's bytes keeps its record's `ID` and `CHECKSUM`, and a body placed as before keeps its `MODEL.CHECKSUM` (c0449, task 4.3). The way back wrote every model under the `ID` of its bytes with `CHECKSUM` 0, so an Altium session that had opened an earlier round trip of NG6011 (public row `-04`) showed that file's model names: Altium seems to serve a model it has opened by its `ID`. The conversion to KiCad carries the identities in the board property `Fenolite.Altium.ModelIds`. No public source or corpus record gives the checksum's rule, so a new model gets the `ID` of its bytes and its name, which no other model's entry holds, and the stand-in 0. Saved checksums are signed in some documents and unsigned in others; both are read and written.
- An Altium model body taken to KiCad and back keeps the heights its source stores (c0449, task 4.3). The way back wrote `STANDOFFHEIGHT` and `OVERALLHEIGHT` from the STEP model's box, so U1 of public row `-04` (NG6011) came back with 8.2677 and 24.6457 mil where the source stores 0 and 10 mil. The conversion to KiCad now carries each placed embedded model's stored heights in the board property `Fenolite.Altium.ModelHeights`, keyed by the model's SHA-256, offset, turns and side; a model that comes back with that key gets them, and a model that is new or changed in KiCad still gets its box's.
- An embedded Altium model is written to KiCad under its own name (c0449, task 4.3). The extracted STEP file was named after the footprint (`<footprint>_<sha12>.step`), so a footprint named after another part made the right model look like the wrong one: in public row `-04` and on NG6011, U1's footprint is named `LM3644YFFR` and its own TPS63050 model was written as `LM3644YFFR_<sha12>.step`. The file is now `<model>_<sha12>.step`, `<model>` the record's `NAME` without its `.step` or `.stp` extension; the footprint's name stays for a model without a name and for the generated models of bodies.
- The source's `Board6` settings and mechanical layer names survive a trip through KiCad (c0464, S-2230 to S-2234). The writer made `Board6` up: `DISPLAYUNIT=1` always, so a metric document came back in mils and a 0.025 mm rule read as 1 mil, mechanical layers named after KiCad layers (`User.1`, `Dwgs.User`), its own green solder mask in the 3D view settings (row `-05` is black), and the hole-shape hash pairs and Situs strategy dropped. The conversion to KiCad now carries every `Board6` key that the writer does not own (identity, frame, stack tables, layer sets and pairs, planes, layer-bound keys and xSignals stay the writer's) as `Board6.Settings`, with the mechanical names; the conversion back merges them, each in its source place. A mechanical layer takes the source's name when the KiCad layer on it is the import's `Mech.<n>` or when nothing lies on it.
- A shelved Altium polygon and one on an internal plane are exact outcomes of the computed pour, not refusals (c0463 tasks 3.3 and 3.4). They pour no copper, as in Altium; the pour census counts them under `exact`, and a later polygon that overlaps one is no longer refused `depends-on-refused`. A test proves that every documented `POUROVERSTYLE`, `ARCRESOLUTION`, `AREATHRESHOLD` and `NECKWIDTHTHRESHOLD` value reads. Shaping texts and pads for `unshaped-copper` and `unshaped-pad` waits for c0460.
- The computed Altium pour pours a polygon whose outline holds arc vertices or repeats a vertex (c0463 tasks 1 and 2, S-2221, S-2222). The import keeps no outline for an arc polygon, so the pour builds the ring from the polygon record: each arc becomes the fewest equal chords within the polygon's `ARCRESOLUTION`, their ends on the arc; the record keeps its arcs. A run of a repeated vertex is kept once, as Altium's saved pours of public rows 02 and 07 show. `outline-arc` stays for an arc without radius or whose ends miss its vertices, and a self-crossing ring still refuses `outline`. The pour census no longer refuses `outline-arc` (12) or `outline` (2): those polygons now wait on c0462's rule scopes (`depends-on-refused` 43, `rule-scope` 21, `rule-unmapped` 9, `connect-scope` 5 over the eight rows).
- The c0462 via-template kit (S-2247, Altium 24.5.2): a Routing Via Style in template mode whose named template Altium does not resolve checks no via, and Altium ignores an authored via-template cache block that has no `TEMPLATE.HASH`. A resolved template is not measured, so such records stay `Unmapped` with `template`.
- Altium rule scopes that read the board map to exact selectors, and the neutral selector has the leaves `zone`, `hole` and `pad_type` (c0462 tasks 2.1, 2.2, 2.4, 3.1, 3.3; S-2212, S-2213). `read.scopedoc.scope_document` gathers what the functions read: copper layers, via spans, nets, differential pairs, classes, components with their pads, polygon names. `OnTopLayer`, `OnBottomLayer`, `OnMid` and `OnOutside` give layer leaves without the objects on Multi-Layer, and `ExistsOnLayer`/`OnLayer` with them (`H-A-RD-PRJ-SCOPE-MULTI`); pair functions give the nets of the pairs; `HasFootprint`, `IsComponent`, `IsNetTieComponent`, `InComponentClass`, `CompPinCount` and the package checks `IsBGA`, `IsSOIC`, `IsLCC` (`read.packages`, `H-A-RD-PRJ-SCOPE-PACKAGE`) give `ref` leaves; `InNamedPolygon`/`IsNamedPolygon` give `zone`, `HoleDiameter`/`HoleSize` `hole` (`H-A-RD-PRJ-SCOPE-UNIT`), `PadIsPlated` `pad_type`. A function that returns parent objects maps only for the rule kind that judges them (`scope.for_kind`), else `object-kind`; xSignals, unions and rooms are refused (`xsignal`, `union`, `room`), as are blind-via layer checks (`via-span`), shared designators (`designator`) and undecidable package checks (`package-check`). `RuleSubject` gains `zone`, `hole` and `pad_type`, filled by the copper check and the computed pour; `fenolite.dsl.select` gains `zone`, `hole` and `pad_type`, and the Python emitter prints a rule expanded from the board with a comment naming the Altium function. The KiCad rules writer writes the leaves as `S.Name`, `S.Pad_Type` and a via `Hole` / pad `Hole_Size_X` interval (task 3.2, S-2213, `H-K-DRU-LEAF-C0462`) and reads back exactly those forms; their probes `dru-cond-zone`, `-pad_type` and `-hole` are `present` on 10.0.6, and the keys stay out of `SELECTOR_SUPPORT` (a reported loss on write, opaque on read) until 9.0.9 is recorded. `tools/altium_scope_oracle.py` makes the kit step of task 5.1 (S-2214): a DelphiScript that counts, per object kind, what Altium's PCB filter selects for each of the 78 distinct mapped scopes of the 8 public rows, from public PCB API names (S-2215) and the `PCB:RunQuery` parameters of a legacy page (S-2216, `UNVERIFIED`), and a `record` step that writes the oracle JSON. `OnMid` on a board without an internal signal layer gives the info `altium.rule.applies-to-nothing` in place of `altium.rule.unmapped`; its record stays `Unmapped` with `no-layer`, so a conversion keeps it. The model schema is `1.1`: the identity migration `1.0 -> 1.1` reads a `1.0` folder, the writer stamps `1.1` on every document, and a `1.0` reader refuses it (`FEN-3002`); the model 0 review records the widening of `Selector.op` by `zone`, `hole` and `pad_type` with its own approval (2026-11-06, coordinator under maintainer delegation), and the freeze tests accept only such a recorded widening that keeps every approved value. The two hole-size scopes of the public rows, `HoleDiameter > 0` on row 05, map in any unit.

This writes the two Hole Size records of convert census row `-05` on 10, which the first commit of task 3.2 reported lost; pour census rows `-04` and `-07` now pour every polygon with an outline, and their M1/M2 failures, the pour's geometry rather than the rules read, are pinned as a known item of task 5.2 (for c0463). `tools/altium_scope_oracle.py` makes the kit step of task 5.1 (S-2214): a DelphiScript that counts, per object kind, what Altium's PCB filter selects for each of the 78 distinct mapped scopes of the 8 public rows, from public PCB API names (S-2215) and the `PCB:RunQuery` parameters of a legacy page (S-2216, `UNVERIFIED`), and a `record` step that writes the oracle JSON. `OnMid` on a board without an internal signal layer gives the info `altium.rule.applies-to-nothing` in place of `altium.rule.unmapped`; its record stays `Unmapped` with `no-layer`, so a conversion keeps it. The two hole-size scopes of the public rows, `HoleDiameter > 0` on row 05, map in any unit.
- `tools/altium_scope_oracle.py` gains a by-hand path for the c0462 task 5.1 kit step: the first run, on Altium 24.5.2.23, selected nothing through `PCB:RunQuery` from the script (every count 0; S-2216 stays `UNVERIFIED`). The new procedure `FenoliteCountSelection` counts the selection a PCB Filter step made, for the scope key written in `<document>.current.txt`, writes `<document>.counts-<n>.txt` and clears the selection; `record` joins a row's per-scope files (a scope holding `|` with none is `skipped`) before its `.counts.txt`.
- Altium rooms are read as rule areas, and `WithinRoom` stays refused (c0462 task 2.3; S-0297, S-2212). `read.rooms` reads each Room Definition record (of a PCB document's `Rules6` or of a rule file): name, side, enabled, confinement and the outline's vertices. A room on the top or bottom side with a straight outline becomes a named rule area on that side's copper layer that restricts nothing, its provenance the record; the record stays a kept record, so an Altium write gives it back as read, and the Altium writer counts the area `changed` under `keep-out` instead of lost. `WithinRoom` of a room the document holds is refused `room-membership` (Altium takes a pad or via by its centre and any other object only wholly inside; the neutral `area` leaf selects by touch), one it does not hold `undefined`: the four scopes of public rows 04 and 07 name `BGA Decoupling`, which neither board holds. That a document's room record holds the rule file's keys is `INFERRED` (`H-A-RD-ROOM`); the exact mapping, a neutral room membership, waits for a public row or a kit sample with a room.
- Altium rule scopes that read the board map to exact selectors, and the neutral selector has the leaves `zone`, `hole` and `pad_type` (c0462 tasks 2.1, 2.2, 2.4, 3.1, 3.3; S-2212, S-2213). `read.scopedoc.scope_document` gathers what the functions read: copper layers, via spans, nets, differential pairs, classes, components with their pads, polygon names. `OnTopLayer`, `OnBottomLayer`, `OnMid` and `OnOutside` give layer leaves without the objects on Multi-Layer, and `ExistsOnLayer`/`OnLayer` with them (`H-A-RD-PRJ-SCOPE-MULTI`); pair functions give the nets of the pairs; `HasFootprint`, `IsComponent`, `IsNetTieComponent`, `InComponentClass`, `CompPinCount` and the package checks `IsBGA`, `IsSOIC`, `IsLCC` (`read.packages`, `H-A-RD-PRJ-SCOPE-PACKAGE`) give `ref` leaves; `InNamedPolygon`/`IsNamedPolygon` give `zone`, `HoleDiameter`/`HoleSize` `hole` (`H-A-RD-PRJ-SCOPE-UNIT`), `PadIsPlated` `pad_type`. The KiCad rules writer writes the leaves as `S.Name`, `S.Pad_Type` and a via `Hole` / pad `Hole_Size_X` interval (task 3.2, S-2213, `H-K-DRU-LEAF-C0462`) and reads back exactly those forms; their probes `dru-cond-zone`, `-pad_type` and `-hole` are `present` on 10.0.6, so `SELECTOR_SUPPORT` holds the keys for KiCad 10 alone; on 9 a rule with one is a reported loss on write, until 9.0.9 is recorded. This writes the two Hole Size records of convert census row `-05` on 10, which the first commit of task 3.2 reported lost; pour census rows `-04` and `-07` now pour every polygon with an outline, and their M1/M2 failures, the pour's geometry rather than the rules read, are pinned as a known item of task 5.2 (for c0463). `tools/altium_scope_oracle.py` makes the kit step of task 5.1 (S-2214): a DelphiScript that counts, per object kind, what Altium's PCB filter selects for each of the 78 distinct mapped scopes of the 8 public rows, from public PCB API names (S-2215) and the `PCB:RunQuery` parameters of a legacy page (S-2216, `UNVERIFIED`), and a `record` step that writes the oracle JSON. `OnMid` on a board without an internal signal layer gives the info `altium.rule.applies-to-nothing` in place of `altium.rule.unmapped`; its record stays `Unmapped` with `no-layer`, so a conversion keeps it.
