The two hole-size scopes of the public rows, `HoleDiameter > 0` on row 05, map in any unit.
- Altium rule scopes are read by Altium's order of precedence, typed (c0462 task 1, S-2211, S-2212). `parse_scope(text, *, document=None)` is a precedence-climbing parser: brackets, `Not`, `^ * / Div Mod And`, `+ - Or Xor`, the comparisons, `&& ||`, left to right. Mixed operators no longer need brackets. `Xor`, `x = True`/`'False'`, `AsMils`/`AsMM` and `Between` are read. A typed reading that is not a set of objects is refused with the reason `type`, naming the operator (`HoleDiameter > 1 And IsPad`), and a number without a unit with `unit`; refusals are `scope.Refusal` strings with a `reason`. `InNet` and `InComponent` take several names, and their `*` and `?` are Altium's wildcards while `[` and `]` match themselves (`H-A-RD-PRJ-SCOPE-WILD`); the design had asked for them escaped, which would select other nets than Altium. `map_rules` passes a `ScopeDocument` through to `parse_scope`.
- OpenSpec change c0465 (S-2240 to S-2249 allocated): `fenolite check` honours an allowed short and an antenna tolerance. Two rule kinds, `short` (its severity says what a short between its two sides is; `ignore` allows it) and `antenna` (`max`, the longest stub that passes), are judged by the copper and DRC stages without removing a finding. c0462's Altium Short-Circuit and Net Antennae records map onto them, and KiCad gets the exact cases and a reported loss for the rest. The hypotheses H-A-RULE-ANTENNA, H-A-RULE-SHORT and H-K-SHORT-ALLOW are registered as pending.
- OpenSpec changes c0462 and c0463 for v0.7 (S-2210 to S-2212, S-2220). c0462 reads every Altium rule scope and rule record that governs copper. It takes Altium's operator precedence (S-2211) and maps the layer, room, pair, xSignal, component and package functions onto exact selectors. It adds the neutral leaves `zone`, `hole` and `pad_type`, reads per-layer, template and matrix keys, and adds a closed table of rule kinds. c0463 makes the computed pour Altium's pour with no Repour. It pours outline arcs, simple-made outlines and hatches, compares with a Repour oracle from the kit, and graduates the pour to `ALTIUM-VERIFIED(kit)`.
- Altium Width and Differential Pairs Routing records read their per-layer values (c0462 task 4.1, S-2248, S-2219). A per-layer key (`TOPLAYER_`, `MIDLAYER<n>_`, `BOTTOMLAYER_` with `MIN`, `PREF` or `MAX` and `WIDTH` or `GAP`) gives one rule `and(scope, layer L)` named `<record>/<L>` at the record's priority on each signal layer whose limits differ from the general ones; a key of a layer the board lacks gives the info `altium.rules.layer-absent`. A profile-driven record (`IMPEDANCEPROFILEDRIVEN=TRUE`) without a value for every signal layer stays unmapped with the new reason `impedance-profile` (3 public pair records); `MINIMP`, `MAXIMP` and `FAVIMP` are read and unused. Two public Width records with values for absent internal layers now map; a record with per-layer keys is kept for an Altium write. A Routing Via Style in template mode (`USEVIATEMPLATES=TRUE`, 4 public records) stays unmapped with the reason `template`: no public source states what Altium's check tests in that mode (task 4.2, H-A-RULE-VIA-TEMPLATE); template keys without it are allowed and unused, so one more public record maps. The reason `matrix-kind` is added for task 4.3. Every rule kind of the public rows now has a reading in the closed table "Rule kinds and their reading" of `rule-file.md` (task 4.4, S-2217, S-2218 and S-2249): neutral; read by another part (`read-elsewhere`: plane, polygon connect, mask and paste records); checked by `fenolite check` itself (`checked`: Short-Circuit, Un-Routed Net, Net Antennae, Un-Poured Polygon, with the warning `altium.rules.check-relaxed` for a record that relaxes its check); or carried with a named reason. Matched Lengths maps onto `diff_pair_skew` or `skew` with its tolerance as the maximum; all 15 public records give their tolerance as a delay and stay unmapped with the new reason `delay-units`. A relaxing record is reported, not honoured: `fenolite check` takes no allowance by net and no dangling tolerance. A Width record with `CHECKCONNECTEDCOPPER=TRUE` (Altium's connected-copper width check) stays carried with the new reason `connected-copper`. Scoped and unscoped mask expansions stay read by the openings (c0440, c0445). A kit in `~/fenolite-altium-checks` asks Altium what its check tests in template mode. Polygon Connect Style scopes are read with the document's `ScopeDocument` and Altium's precedence (task 4.5, part): a scope of mixed operators now governs instead of refusing the pour with `connect-scope`.
- Flow A keeps the Altium rule records of a build that writes conditions in parentheses and board minimums (c0457 follow-up, S-2162). A build writes `(A.Type == 'Via' && A.NetClass == 'X')` where the conversion wrote it bare, so 11 untouched Routing Via Style rules were refused as `SOURCE_RULE_LOST`. The carrier (schema `fenolite.altium-rules.v2`) now keys a condition by its parsed form, else by its text without balanced outer parentheses; version 1 still reads. The board minimums an import writes (`design.rules.minimum`, built as `fenolite_0_min_*`) are no longer lowered to Altium rules: one that another unconditioned rule already holds adds nothing, any other is a reported loss (`BOARD_MINIMUM`), never a refusal.
- Altium classes of every kind, xSignals and differential pairs survive the round trip through KiCad, and an internal plane is counted lost on the way to KiCad (c0461, S-2200 to S-2204). The conversion carries every `Classes6` record that is no net class and no super class (`Classes6.Kept`; c0450's `Classes6.Component` still reads), the xSignal classes of `SignalClasses`, the `PINPAIR` keys of `Board6` and `DifferentialPairs6`; the conversion back writes them byte for byte, a differential pair only when the document holds its nets, and no longer adds its own sheet classes or KiCad's empty `Default` net class beside them. A class of another kind may share a net class's name (`-01`: `CELL` and `Cell`). Each `Split Plane` polygon of a plane in the chain is a `plane` loss of the conversion to KiCad (8 in public row 01, 5 in row 02): KiCad's own import makes it an unfilled zone on a `power` layer, Fenolite's conversion makes none. The kit notes c0444's BMS pass b, which hangs Altium, as a hazard.
- Archived the OpenSpec changes that lote-28's green CI completes (c0420 to c0424, c0426, c0428 to c0437, c0439 to c0443, c0445, c0447, c0448 and c0451); their CI-only tasks cite run 42229338778 on abe1de83f.
- A built project keeps its own 3D models (c0458, S-2170). `fenolite build` copies each model file that a vendored footprint names as `${KIPRJMOD}/<rel>` and the design script's folder holds to `<rel>` in the built project. Before, a design imported from Altium named `${KIPRJMOD}/<library>.3dshapes/…` while the files stayed beside the script, so KiCad (15 of 15 not found by `kicad-cli pcb export step`) and `convert --to altium` (183 of 183 lost on the maintainer's board) found none of them. Official-library models (`${KICAD*}` paths) are still never copied. `convert --to altium` now reads a model path as KiCad does: absolute paths and paths that leave the project folder are followed, and an absent file there is lost as "the model file is not found at its path".
- Carried Altium rule records match the rules file by content, and an edit in KiCad is a change (c0457, S-2160, S-2161). Change c0434 keyed each rule list by its text, its name included. A build of an imported design names its rules again, so flow A (import to Python, one clearance edited, build, convert to Altium) refused 11 untouched rules as `SOURCE_RULE_LOST`. The property now keys each list by its content and its shape, without the name, the order of its children or the spaces of a condition (schema `fenolite.altium-rules.v1`; version 0 still reads). A copy of a kept record's rule (a board minimum the import made) is not written again. A record whose limits alone were edited keeps its name, priority, scopes and enabled state with KiCad's limits; any other edit is written as a rule of its kind; both count `changed`. The computed pour of the way back still refuses the polygons a Clearance record of unread scope could govern: the pour of the source refuses the same ones (public row -04: 9 of 9), lote-27 poured them only because it lost the record, and a pour that ignores the record or bounds its gap differs from the regions the document saves by 3.5 to 7.4 times the M1 allowance (S-2160).
- The KiCad import checks compare a net tie's copper again (c0431 follow-up, S-2083). KiCad's import keeps the copper of an Altium Net Tie component in its footprint: a track as a copper line, a region as a custom pad without a number or a net whose primitive is the region. `altium_import.import_design` read the imported board without projecting footprint items, and `equivalent`'s net-tie comparison did not look at pads, so public row `altium-third-party-pcbdoc-01` (RT-A3 oracle and triangle level 3) and demo boards 11, 13 and 16 (convert triangle) reported `net-tie-copper` on Fenolite's side only. `import_design` now projects the footprint graphics and texts, and the comparison counts the primitives of an unnumbered, netless custom pad as footprint copper.
- The extension key `altium`/`stroke` of c0450 is recorded as approved in the model 0 review, and the curve-error test reads the board's appended fields in order after c0445's `mask_margin` (lote-28).
- The Altium conversion census counts c0450's `altium-storage` losses (1, 0, 2, 0, 1, 1, 2 and 5 in public rows 01 to 08), and the Altium backend's import rule lets `fills.py` build its fill marker with `uuid` (lote-28).
- Batch fixes after lote-27's CI (lote-28). The computed pour reads a polygon cut-out whose record repeats a vertex: it raised `geometry.degenerate` on public rows 02 and 06 since lote-26; the pour census is repinned now that a cut-out is taken out of the area instead of refusing its polygon (`polygon-cutout`), so those polygons are refused at the next criteria. The Altium writer writes no `WideStrings6` entry for an empty text (c0451): kicad-cli 10.0.6 refused the whole document ("WideStrings6 stream is not fully parsed"), and a reader takes the record's empty short text. Public row 06 has no component cut-out annotation since c0443.
- The Altium edit session's `flip` writes the frame Altium gives a part on the other side (c0453, S-2120 to S-2122). Altium places a bottom part as its footprint mirrored in Y and then turned by `ROTATION`, as all 235 bottom parts with asymmetric pads of the public rows read, and its own flip of `D1` (Part J case J9) writes `ROTATION` 180 and pad rotations 180. The session mirrors in X about the origin and wrote `ROTATION` −R and pad rotations −r, a frame that reads the footprint turned by a half turn; after an Altium save the pads and the designator of such parts came back on the old side. The flip now writes 180 − R and 180 − r, a body's `BODYPROJECTION` follows the side. A flip back restores every record. The kit run (S-2123) holds after an Altium save. Altium's own flip of a body keeps `MODEL.3D.ROTZ` and `MODEL.2D.ROTATION` at the top body's values and writes `TEXTUREROTATION` 0 as 360, and its save turned the 180 and 180 the flip first wrote back to 0 and 0. The flip now writes the body as Altium does (task 3.3): on the way down `MODEL.3D.ROTZ` and `MODEL.2D.ROTATION` become 0, as Altium's own flip writes them for any top value and its save writes them on any bottom body (S-2124), `TEXTUREROTATION` 0 becomes 360 and any other is kept. Altium's save leaves the flipped body byte for byte (S-2124).
- `convert --to altium` embeds the 3D models of a KiCad board (c0449, S-2080 to S-2082). Each `model` child of a footprint that names a STEP file of the project (`${KIPRJMOD}/…` or a relative path), unscaled, is written into the document's `Models` storage once per file and named by a model body of the footprint's component, at the inverse of the pose that the Altium → KiCad direction writes, with the placed model's box as its outline and heights (`backends.stepbox.model_box`, Fenolite's own STEP box reader). The report counts each child under `model`: written, changed when KiCad hides it, or lost with its reason (missing file, another path variable, `kicad-embed://`, no STEP text, scaled, no edge to measure). Before, every model of a KiCad board was dropped without a word, so an Altium board taken to KiCad and back came back without its models; a board whose models live outside the project (`${KICAD9_3DMODEL_DIR}/…`) now reports them as `model` losses with a `convert.lossy` warning (16 of the 18 demo boards of the census ledger), with `--altium-bodies off` every model is lost with the reason of the bodies; on the eight public rows all 472 model bodies now come back with the same model, pose and side. Altium's acceptance of `CHECKSUM=0` and of Fenolite's model GUIDs is not yet seen.
- Altium union members and TrueType texts keep their references through KiCad (c0460, S-2190). A via, a region and a component keep their union index, carried by the item's KiCad uuid as `Fenolite.Altium.UnionMembers` and written back. A text of font type 1 is read with its font as its face, bold and italic, and the Altium writer writes a text with a face as a TrueType text instead of refusing it. `SmartUnions` holds placements in the source's frame and is counted lost.
- A conversion from Altium to KiCad no longer carries the source's `BoardRegions` and `CustomMaskShapes` storages and counts each lost when it holds data (c0459, S-2180 to S-2182). The board's layer stack region lies in the source's frame and names the source's stack, which the round trip moves and writes again; in Altium Designer the copy left every polygon of public rows `-04` and `-08` empty after a repour, with a Board Outline Clearance collision on almost every object; without it rows `-04` and `-08` pour every polygon. `CustomMaskShapes` goes as the cautious choice: no public row fills it, so whether it holds coordinates is not known.
- A keep-out fill of copper of an Altium board comes back from KiCad as a keep-out fill with its restriction byte, not a keep-out region (c0455, S-2140). Its rule area carries its corners and byte in its name (`Altium.KeepOut/flag<r>/fill/…`); an area edited in KiCad stays a region. Row `-08` gives its 11 keep-out fills back.
- Altium records that the round trip through KiCad lost come back (c0450, S-2090 to S-2093). A fill keeps a KiCad uuid tagged as an Altium fill (`altfil`), and the Altium writer writes a tagged rectangle as a fill record of the 50-byte form, free or of its component, where it wrote a region. `UnionNames`, `BoardRegions`, `EmbeddedFonts6`, `CustomMaskShapes` and the component classes of `Classes6` travel as KiCad board properties `Fenolite.Altium.<storage>` (base64 of zlib), written in one pass with the rule records through the shared board carrier of c0434, and are written back byte for byte. `PrimitiveGuids` and the saved violations are counted lost under `altium-storage`. A keep-out track or arc of copper carries its stroke and restriction byte in its rule area's name (`Altium.KeepOut/flag<r>/…`) and comes back as a keep-out track or arc of the long form, where it came back as a region. The second ring of a c0442 circle now pairs with its first, so a plain keep-out circle no longer leaves a keep-out region beside its arc.
- Batch fixes after lote-26's CI (lote-27). `equivalent` compares copper without a net (`copper-no-net`, c0439) in the relative frame, moved by level 4's translation, and counts a piece a side holds twice once: against KiCad's own importer, public row 03 gave 8 false differences. The Altium writer computes an outline arc vertex (c0441) in integers (`core.trig.angle_of`), so no module of the Altium backend imports `math`. A conversion moves the board by whole document units only when its outline lies on them, as a board read from a PCB document does; any other board is anchored as a build anchors it, so a build and a write of one design agree again.
- The KiCad demo conversion census is repinned after c0431, c0441 and c0444 (lote-27): boards 11, 13 and 16 no longer lose outline arcs or net-tie copper, and `equivalent` now reports the copper that reaches a pad the round trip lost (`route-connectivity`) and compares a net tie's copper (`net-tie-copper`), each explained by the conversion report.
- Speed up `fenolite check` on boards with many arcs (c0437). The copper check spent most of its time measuring, with exact square-root arithmetic, the distance from an arc to every segment of a nearby fill or zone ring. Float bounds now leave out a segment only when it is farther by a slack of 1 nm plus 10⁻⁹ of the coordinates, far above their rounding, so every decision stays exact. The touch and clearance tests no longer compute a distance: they stop at the first segment within the threshold. On the KiCad conversion of public row `altium-third-party-pcbdoc-08` (544 parts) the copper stage takes 47.8 s instead of 102.4 s, and on row `-01` 7.2 s instead of 9.6 s, with the same findings.
- The copper of an Altium component keeps its net through KiCad (c0454, S-2130, S-2131). The import gives a component's copper graphic its `net_id`. The KiCad writer writes it as `(net …)` on the `fp_*`, where it used to clear it. `with_graphic_nets` reads it back for footprints. The Altium writer writes a netted copper graphic of any footprint as primitives of its component with that net, where only net-tie footprints kept copper (c0431). On public row `-01`, the regions of `L3` and the fills of `Q2` and `Q6` come back with their nets, where the round trip lost all four (the fills as regions until c0450).
- c0442 task 2.2: Altium Designer 24.5 opens the round trip of public row `-01` with its keep-out lines as tracks and arcs and no keep-out region, and DRC adds no violation (S-2011).
- A conversion to Altium writes only the rules of its source (c0434, S-1930, S-1931). The writer added a Clearance, Width and Routing Via Style record per net class and an `All` default per kind (change c0038). So public row -08 came back from KiCad with 18 Clearance, 15 Width and 15 Routing Via Style records where the source holds 4, 1 and 3, and Altium reported 2292 Width violations. A KiCad project gives every class KiCad's defaults, which no source record held. A write of a model now writes no class track width or via size, which KiCad does not check. It writes the class clearances only when no clearance rule on every object overrides them (`PcbDocSpec.class_kinds`). It counts a net class with a value left out `changed`. A build keeps every class rule. A rule lacking a limit stays refused, as before. The v0.3 acceptance tool states the preferred via hole of its Altium model (0.3 mm), which a KiCad `via_drill` rule cannot hold; a default-class record stood in for it before. The refusal without `--allow-lossy` (`FEN-7001`) now lists every loss that consent would accept, not only the refused kinds, and its message names the others.
- A free pad of an Altium board comes back from KiCad as a free pad (c0447, S-2060, S-2061). The Altium → KiCad conversion links the footprint of a free pad `<project>:FREE_<footprint id>` (`libnames.FREE_PAD_PREFIX`), and the Altium writer knew a free pad only without a link, so it wrote a component with designator `''`, whose Designator and Comment texts at (-1000, -1000) mil broke Board Outline Clearance: NG6011 0 → 4 and 4 of DM6010's 28 in Altium 24.5. The writer now also writes a footprint with no reference, one pad and a `FREE_` link as a free pad (`lower.converted_free_pad`, counted changed with `lower.FREE_PAD_REASON`). The other 24 of DM6010 are silk tracks across the edge: the source's priority-1 Board Outline Clearance has a text-only object matrix, which no KiCad rule holds, so the round trip leaves its `All` 5.9 mil rule in force (`pcb-copper.md`); its mapping waits for a measurement of what the matrix exempts.
- `build --target altium` writes what `convert --to altium` writes for three items (c0448, S-2070 to S-2079). A net-tie footprint with copper, such as an `F.Cu` line joining its pads, is no longer refused with `altium.footprint-unsupported`, which had left the whole PCB document out: the library writes the copper on `F.Cu` and `B.Cu`, and the document writes it as a track, arc or region of the Net Tie component (`COMPONENTKIND` 4), as c0431 does for a conversion. A pad's own mask or paste margin, which c0440's pad writer writes as a manual expansion, is no longer reported as dropped. An arc of the board outline is written as one arc vertex, as c0441 does for a conversion; before, the build wrote no PCB document for such a board (public row `altium-third-party-pcbdoc-06` after `import --to python`). A board ring of arcs with fewer than three vertices, such as a round board, still plans no document. An authored test builds and converts one board and finds the same net-tie copper, pad masks and outline in both documents.
- A conversion from Altium to KiCad and back writes every rule record of the source as it was read: name, priority, scopes, enabled state and limits (c0434, S-1931). The conversion to KiCad writes them into the board property `Fenolite.Altium.Rules6` (`convert.rulecarrier`): each record with a hash of each rules-file rule list it gave. The shared `backends.kicad.boardcarrier` writes and reads such `Fenolite.Altium.*` properties, zlib and base64, after `setup`; KiCad 10.0.6 keeps them through a re-save (H-K-FENOLITE-PROPS). The way back writes each record whose rule lists are unchanged, or that gave none, as read, so the Height, Minimum Solder Mask Sliver, disabled and other records KiCad has no rule for come back, and lowers only the rules no such record accounts for, with the next priority of their kind. Public row -08 comes back with its 56 records equal to the source's. A rule of an edited source record that Altium cannot hold is a loss of reason `SOURCE_RULE_LOST`, which needs `--allow-lossy`.
- A conversion keeps the solder-mask opening of vias (c0445, S-2040 to S-2043). On public row -01 the vias open by a rule of scope `IsVia` of 1 mil from the hole, 12 mil across; KiCad opened them by a board margin of 0 (20 mil) and the way back wrote 4 mil from the rules with no rule (28 mil), and Altium reported 1655 mask slivers where the source has 103. New field `Board.mask_margin`, written by KiCad as `pad_to_mask_clearance`, the one value KiCad 10 holds for a via's opening (a rules-file `solder_mask_expansion` does not reach a via). A conversion to KiCad gives the board the most frequent via opening and counts the other vias `changed` under `via`; pads without their own margin get 0, so they open as before. A conversion to Altium writes that margin as every via's own expansion (manual mode). Builds write the same bytes as before.
- An Altium polygon pour takes its island and neck thresholds from its zone, and the Altium reader gives them to the zone (c0446). The writer gave every pour `REMOVEISLANDSBYAREA=TRUE` with 2500 mil² and a 5 mil neck. Altium removes a piece below that area whatever it connects, which a KiCad zone never does. Now a zone with settings other than the defaults gets its own: area removal only when it removes islands below an area, with that area, and its minimum width as the neck width. A zone with the default settings keeps the bytes the kit verified (2500 mil², 5 mil), and the reader maps those Altium defaults back to the default settings. The reader projects `REMOVEDEAD`, `REMOVEISLANDSBYAREA`, `AREATHRESHOLD`, `REMOVENECKS` and `NECKWIDTHTHRESHOLD` into `Zone.settings`, so a round trip through KiCad keeps them: public row 06 comes back with its sources' 0.1 mm² and 0.1/0.15 mm. Level 5 of that round trip with the computed pour still differs on the pour nets, so the thresholds were not what split them.
- An Altium board taken to KiCad and back keeps its texts, values and library links (c0451, S-2100, S-2101). The Altium writer writes the comment record of a component whose model places a `Value` field also when the comment is empty (BMS lost four such fields), and a footprint text without characters. The Altium → KiCad conversion writes each component's source footprint and symbol links as the hidden footprint properties `Altium Footprint` and `Altium Symbol` (`convert.altium_links`); the KiCad → Altium conversion gives them back while the footprint is still the one derived from the source pattern, and ships one derived `.PcbLib` and `.SchLib` per source library, named as the source names it (`project.write_project(..., extra_footprints=...)`); every public row used to come back with the project's name as every library. A special string that names no project parameter is written to KiCad as `${NAME}`, a variable the project does not define; the way back writes `${NAME}` as `.NAME` (or `'.NAME'` inside other text), KiCad's predefined board variables as the matching special strings, and the project's text variables as `[Parameter<n>]` entries, which it did not write before (the blink sample came back with the text `${REV_CODE}`). `lower.write_design` takes `parameters`.
- `equivalent` level 5 sees copper that reaches a pad the other side lacks (c0444, S-2030). It compared only the pads both sides hold, so a track that ended on a pad a conversion lost joined the same pad set on both sides. Now such a pad counts on its side in a piece with copper, and the net gives `equiv.route-connectivity`; a lone pad stays a level-2 and level-3 difference. In the Altium → KiCad → Altium round trip of public rows 01 and 06, the lost pads were rounded rectangles whose corner ratio the KiCad writer left out, which c0441 fixes. On that base the Altium writer's fixed polygon thresholds (`REMOVEISLANDSBYAREA=TRUE`, `AREATHRESHOLD` 2500 mil²) are the remaining suspect for the antennae on vias; they are not changed here.
- The computed Altium pour takes a polygon cut-out (`KIND=1`) out of the polygon's area instead of refusing the polygon (lote-26). Since c0439 the round trip writes a pour-only rule area as such a cut-out, and the engine refused every polygon whose reach held one, so a board read back from KiCad was written with unpoured polygons. A cut-out on Multi-Layer applies to every copper layer. The reason `polygon-cutout` now means the cut-out could not be subtracted. Test expectations follow c0427, c0428, c0433 and c0435 on public rows 03 and 08 and on the slot round trip.
- `equivalent` treats a non-plated pad no larger than its round hole as holding no copper on any layer, so the layers it names do not count (lote-26). KiCad names `F&B.Cu` for such a mounting hole and an Altium multi-layer pad names every copper layer; since c0433 keeps these pads in their component, public demo row 01 gave 9 false `pad-copper` differences. The stray-copper comparison of c0439 reaches the KiCad graphic nets through `fenolite.model.graphicnets`, a hook the KiCad package registers, so `checks.equivalence` imports no backend.
- Flow B (an Altium PCB document converted to KiCad and back) keeps what it read (c0435, S-1940 to S-1949). An arc whose three points lie on one line is written to Altium as the track it draws (`changed`, not lost), so no route length goes missing. A generated hole footprint (`Fenolite_Holes:…`) is written back as the free hole it came from, not as a component without a reference, so `equivalent` no longer reports `ref-ambiguous` for board holes. `equivalent` compares footprint names after the rewrite the conversion gives a library entry (`core.libnames.library_name`), so `R/0603` and `R_0603` are no `footprint-name` difference.
- A KiCad footprint's pads without a number stay in their Altium component (c0433). Since c0160 the writer moved each one out as a free pad and left it out of the derived library, so the read-back footprint lacked them: `equivalent` reported `equiv.pad-missing` at level 3 and `equiv.ref-ambiguous` at level 1, and no `lost` row covered it. Each such pad is now its own pad record of the component with an empty designator and its own net, in the PCB document and in the PcbLib. Documents saved by Altium hold such pads, two in one component in two public boards (S-0172, S-0200, S-0601). The reason "the pad number is empty, so it is written as a free pad at its board position" is gone, and RT-A3 no longer rebuilds detached pads. A pad that the writer cannot hold is still counted `lost`.
- A board cut-out that belongs to an Altium component stays a cut-out of that component through KiCad (c0443; S-2020). The import read such a region (`ISBOARDCUTOUT=TRUE`) as a drawing of the Keep-Out layer, so KiCad got a rule area that forbids everything and no board edge there, and the round trip wrote a flagged keep-out region. It is now a closed `Edge.Cuts` outline of the footprint, which KiCad takes as an edge of the board, and the Altium writer writes a footprint's closed `Edge.Cuts` outline as a board cut-out region with the component's index, once: the same ring among the board's converted cut-outs is not written again. On the public row `-06` the two cut-outs of a connector come back as cut-outs, not keep-outs. Altium Designer 24.5 opens the round trip of row `-06` with the two cut-outs of the component as its board cut-outs and no new rule violation (S-2141).
- A round trip through KiCad no longer turns the plain keep-out lines of the Altium Keep-Out layer into keep-out regions (c0442; S-2010). A track or arc there without the keep-out flag becomes, in KiCad, the rule area of the band its width covers; written back, that area was a flagged region, which a rule scoped to regions selects and one scoped to tracks does not. The conversion now names each such area with its stroke (`Altium.KeepOut/line/…`, `/arc/…`, `/circle/…`), a name that the KiCad file and `import --to python` keep, and the Altium writer gives an unchanged marked area back as a track or an arc of the Keep-Out layer without the flag, with its owner and width. An area edited in KiCad (another outline or restriction) stays a keep-out region. On the public row `-01` the 12 tracks and 8 arcs come back as tracks and arcs, where the round trip wrote 20 regions. `equivalent` compares the `form` of two keep-out drawings, `stroke` or `area`, as a `keepout-field` difference.
- A round trip through KiCad or `import --to python` no longer turns Altium polygon cut-outs into keep-outs, or netted copper shapes into copper of no net (c0439, takes in c0438; S-1980, S-1981). The import reads a polygon cut-out (`KIND=1`) as a rule area that forbids poured copper alone. The writers wrote every rule area as a keep-out region, on the Keep-Out layer when it covered every copper layer, and Altium's clearance rules check a keep-out against every track, pad and via. Such a rule area is now written as a `KIND=1` region without the keep-out flag: on Multi-Layer when it covers every copper layer, else once per signal layer, owned by its component when it belongs to a footprint. This is the form the public documents hold. On the public row `-06` the round trip wrote 13 free keep-out regions and now writes none. A rule area that forbids more stays a keep-out. `convert --to altium` also gives each copper graphic the net its KiCad slot names, as `build` already did; before, every netted copper shape was written with no net. `equivalent` gains the level 5 kind `copper-no-net` (`equiv.copper-no-net`): a piece of copper without a net that one side holds and the other does not. The test boards are authored fixtures.
- `place` can move parts on a board converted from Altium (c0432, S-1910 to S-1919). Only the findings a move makes refuse it: the legality check also runs on the board before the moves, and an error found there too, on parts the command did not move, is the new warning `place.existing`. Before, overlaps and parts in a cut-out that the board already had refused every move. `place.locked` now hints that a converted or imported board keeps each part's lock and that `--force` moves the part. `--strategy group` (`--shift`, `--turn`) drags the copper on the group's pads: a via in a moved pad moves with it, and a track end on a moved pad or on such a via follows, so the group no longer leaves its copper behind and fails the copper check on it (`place.group-failed`). Tests use the built blink and the authored Altium `routed` sample converted to KiCad; flip through `--move` works on both sides there.
- `convert --to altium` keeps the copper of a net-tie footprint (c0431, S-1900). A KiCad footprint with `net_tie_pad_groups` used to lose its copper graphic (for example the F.Cu line that joins its two pads) as a refused `footprint-copper` loss, and with `--allow-lossy` the tie was open in the PcbDoc. The graphic is now written as a track, arc or region of the Net Tie component (`COMPONENTKIND` 4) on its signal layer, without a net. A copper graphic of any other footprint, or one on a plane, is still refused. `equivalent` gains the level 3 kind `net-tie-copper` (`equiv.net-tie-copper`): it compares a net-tie footprint's copper in the footprint's frame, so an open tie is a difference even though each pad keeps its net and levels 2 and 5 are unchanged; a lost `footprint-copper` row explains it. An agent's run in Altium 24.5 opens the written tie as a Net Tie with no message, and its default DRC finds no short on it (S-1901; the level stays INFERRED). The test board is an authored fixture.
- `convert --to altium` no longer writes unpoured polygons without saying so (c0436). Without the `geo` extra, the default pour mode `computed` used to fall back to the polygon outline with one `altium.pour-unavailable` warning and exit 0. It now refuses like an explicit `--altium-pour computed`: exit 6, `FEN-6001`, nothing planned, with a hint to install the extra (`pip install 'fenolite[geo]'` or `uv sync --extra geo`) or pass `--altium-pour outline`. `fenolite.convert.convert_project` raises `BackendUnavailable` the same way. `fenolite build --target altium` keeps its fallback.
- Keep each pad's solder-mask expansion through a conversion between Altium and KiCad, and compare it in `equivalent` (c0440, S-1990, S-1991). The conversion to KiCad emptied each pad's Altium bag before it read the pad's mask mode, so every pad took the rule's expansion, a manual 0 included; and the Altium writer wrote every pad "from the rules", leaving a pad's own margin out. A board taken from Altium to KiCad and back so lost its manual pad masks, and Altium's check found solder mask slivers between fine-pitch pads. Now a manual expansion is kept (0 too), the Altium writer writes a pad's own margin as a manual expansion, and level 3 of `equivalent` gives `pad-mask` when the expansions differ. A pad that followed a rule comes back manual with the rule's value, because KiCad has no rule-or-manual mode.
- An Altium board taken to KiCad and back keeps its shape and its connections (c0441, S-2000 to S-2009). The Altium writer writes an arc of the board outline as one arc vertex, no longer as two straight edges through its mid (BMS, NG6011 and DM6010_R2M2E2 lost 8, 4 and 2 arcs, so copper near a rounded corner broke the board-outline clearance). A conversion to Altium writes a via whose hole equals its diameter (`lower.write_design(..., full_drill=True)`; 48 of 242 vias of foc.PcbDoc were left out), and the KiCad board writer writes the corner ratio of every created rounded-rectangle pad (69 pads of PCB1.PcbDoc came back without one and were left out); the track ends on those vias and pads were the net antennae. A conversion to Altium gives a copper graphic the net its KiCad `(net …)` names (`fpitems.with_graphic_nets`, as a build does): the 45 netted free copper regions of BMS.PcbDoc came back with No Net, and Altium found the vias on them antennae. A conversion to Altium moves the board by whole document units (`Frame.on_units`), so the round trip is one exact translation. `equivalent` level 7 compares the board shape in the relative frame: side b's outline and `Edge.Cuts` drawings are moved back by level 4's translation.
- Imports and conversions no longer report identity differences that come from Fenolite (c0425). Level 2 compares a pad number on a net and on no net through the nets of the other pins, so a conversion that keeps both pads is equal, and an empty part of a label prints as `no net`. The import counts a pad on no net beside a netted pad of its number as `pad-net-conflict`, because the DSL joins every pad of a pin; that row explains the self-check's level 2 difference. A schematic lost for duplicate designators now explains the level 1 `ref-ambiguous` at each designator the source circuit holds more than once. A footprint variant of the library derive (`fenolite library`, and the import's vendored library) takes the next suffix that no footprint of the board holds, so two footprints and their symbols no longer share a name; that collision had failed an import's self-check with `build.unknown-pin`. Every case is an authored fixture.
- Make a conversion's issues agree with its report (c0429, S-1880 to S-1889). `convert` now gives one `convert.lossy` warning for every report row with lost items, whatever its loss class. Before, a `report` kind such as `rule`, `body`, `model`, `schematic` or `graphic` could lose items with no issue. Only a `refuse` kind's loss still needs `--allow-lossy` (`FEN-7001`). `fenolite.convert.report_issues` builds the `convert.lossy`, `convert.changed` and `convert.kept` issues of a report, one per row with that outcome; a test pins this for every kind of `KINDS` and the downgrade kinds. `import --to python` of an Altium source now gives the conversion's report as `result.report`, with the same rows as `convert` (`null` for a KiCad source), and refuses as `convert` does, with the same issues. `fenolite.import.v0` gains the optional `report`, and `frozen-v0.json` is refrozen.
- `import --to python` rebuilds what it reads (c0426). A derived footprint variant no longer takes the name of another footprint of the board: a converted Altium project library holds its own `<name>_2` footprints, and both were written to one file, so a part built with another footprint's pads (nanometres or more off, another size, the other side, an island split off). A variant now takes the next free `<name>_<n>`, in `fenolite library` too. `via_table`, `via` and the copper build accept a via whose drill equals its diameter (a via without a ring), and refuse a larger drill with "larger than the diameter" (`kicad.copper.bad-size`).
- `import --to python` leaves fewer items out (c0427, S-1860 to S-1869). A rule the DSL cannot say (an undeclared class, an unwritable selector) is kept as written: every rule of its constraint kind becomes a `design.verbatim(..., target="kicad_dru")` item in file order, so the import no longer emits a `design.rules` call that does not build. `design.rules.rule()` takes a negative `silk_clearance` minimum, which KiCad 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 read (`H-K-DRU-NEG-SILK`). A net only a copper graphic names gets a net variable; a drawing may be on `F.Paste` or `B.Paste` (`H-K-PASTE-DRAWING`). The Altium reader reads a text's CR LF as a line feed (`H-A-TEXT-CRLF`) and cuts the board shape by a board cut-out that crosses it (`altium.import.cutout-notch`, `H-A-CUTOUT-NOTCH`). Public Altium rows 03 to 07 now import as code with nothing left out; `tests/corpus/test_altium_import_python.py` pins every public row.
- Remove the new losses of an Altium conversion to KiCad (c0428, S-1870, S-1871). Embedded board arrays turned by 180 or 270 degrees are placed. So are arrays whose board's origin is off its shape's corner: by the origin when linked, else by the corner. Both rules are inferred, and the report says so. A referenced document is also looked for relative to the project's folder, by its file name, and at its stored absolute path. Copies carry their zones' arc outlines, their models and the board's cut-outs. Drill guide and drill drawing layers take KiCad user rows. Duplicate designators no longer drop the schematic: the extra parts are written `<ref>_<n>`. `.Layer_Name`, `.Pcb_File_Name`, `.Pcb_File_Name_No_Path` and `.Print_Date` become `${LAYER}`, `${FILEPATH}`, `${FILENAME}` and `${CURRENT_DATE}`. A board document converted alone takes its project's parameters. A zone whose record encloses no area pours no copper: it is left out as a change, with its own reason.
- Fix the Altium edit `flip` of a part on a board Altium saved (c0430, S-1890 to S-1892). Altium showed a flipped part's designator and comment on Top Overlay, because their saved text records kept the long layer id of Top Overlay at offset 226. A saved SMD pad kept the long id of Top Layer at offset 114 in the same way. `flip`, `remap_layer` and every layer move of the edit session now change the long layer id of a text and of a pad with its layer byte. Fenolite's flip of the kit board now matches Altium's own flip of `D1` in layer, long id, mirror flag and place. A flip now swaps mechanical layers only between the pairs the board declares in `MECHPAIR` keys, by long layer id (Mechanical 17 to 32 included). Other mechanical layers stay put, as Altium's own flip leaves them; before, the flip always moved Mechanical 13 to 14 and 15 to 16. The Altium and KiCad writers already put a bottom part's designator and comment on the bottom overlay, mirrored. Tests now pin the Altium write and `fenolite build --target altium` of a part placed with `side="bottom"`.
- Record Part J phase B run again on 24.5 after the writer fix (c0389 task 4.2, S-1612, agent run in AD 24.5.2.23, kit built at `d45e6533`). All nine PCB edit operations pass, `remap_layer` and `set_rule` included, and the three schematic edits show as expected. No dialog or message showed, and every listing is the same after save and reopen. The fault files J26–J28 and J32 behave as in S-1588. J25 and J29–J31 are not executed, because there is no sample, and J33 was not run as written. No level moves, every gate stays off, and the task stays open.
- Record c0389 task 2.6 on 24.5 (S-1611, agent run in AD 24.5.2.23) and J4 redone through the panel (task 4.1, S-1610). OutJob: the job of `blink_routed` writes 12 Gerber layer files with the extensions of its 12 entries, and `board6` writes 16, `.GP1` being the internal plane. The Gerber setup's first entry is a Board Outline row, `Gerber Board Profile`, off by default; turned on, it writes `.GM` and saves `PlotBoardProfile=True` outside `Plot.Set`. Hierarchy modes: Automatic 0, Flat 1, Hierarchical 2, Strict Hierarchical 4, Global 3. Rule export: one rule per line, ended by `B6` and CR LF, no byte-order mark. J4: `R1` moves alone to (14, 20) and the free tracks stay; the J04 file of S-1577 did not contain that move. Task 4.1 is closed; no level moves.
- Record the Part J panel redo on 24.5 (c0389 task 4.1, S-1609, agent run in AD 24.5.2.23 on the phase A bytes of S-1577). J7, J9, J14, J18, J19 and J21 are redone through the Properties panel with the outcomes of their scripted edits. J4's free tracks stay, but the S-1577 J4 file holds `R1` and `R2` at X 26 mm, not `R1` alone at 14 mm. Levels unchanged.
- `equivalent` between an Altium board and its KiCad conversion reports what differs, not how each backend writes it (c0421, S-1800 to S-1809). Across backends each side is completed first: a KiCad project side gets its net classes, its `.kicad_dru` rules and its footprint graphics and texts; an Altium side gets its arc-vertex zone outlines and the zone connection and clearance its rules govern. The native project rule context runs only between two KiCad sides, so public rows 01, 03 and 08 no longer refuse with FEN-3004. New pairings, each with an authored negative case: rules written as `fenolite_<priority>_<slug>`, rules a conversion keeps as declared format limits (`kept_record`, listed as excluded `declared-limit`), layers by user name (`User.<n>` named `Mech.<k>`), keep-out drawings against the rule areas of their outline or band, `Edge.Cuts` lines and arcs against a polygon of the same ring, board-only NPTH hole footprints against board holes, and in strict level 5 an arc whose mid lies within the length tolerance of a track's chord. `compare_designs(..., across=True)`, which `equivalent` sets for two backends, leaves out what one backend never states: unstated mask, paste and silkscreen stack-up layers, stack-up name case, member-less classes and unset class fields, unused non-copper layers, the mechanical/user kind, non-copper layer order, hidden one-side footprint fields, and fields the other side holds as component parameters. On the public Altium rows 02, 04, 05, 06 and 07 the differences fall from 612, 395, 281, 1096 and 380 to 13, 21, 1, 26 and 25; what is left is what the conversion writes differently or not at all. Sides of one backend compare as before.
- Altium bodies and 3D models reach KiCad with their pose (c0423). `convert --to kicad` places every embedded model. Before, a model turned about X or Y or on a bottom-side part was a `model` loss. The placement solves KiCad's model pose, measured on 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 with `pcb export stl` (`H-K-MODEL-POSE`, KICAD-VERIFIED, S-1820): the turn about X first, then Y, then Z, each by the negated `rotate` value, the offset after the turns, and a half turn about X for a bottom-side part. Each component body that no embedded model carries becomes a STEP file of Fenolite's own writer, beside the library: an extruded body its prism, a cylinder a faceted cylinder and a sphere a faceted sphere (`backends.step.write_solids`, sagitta ≤ 10 µm), read by type from the record (`models3d.body_forms`). A shape that does not fit its outline becomes the prism over the outline. Bodies are now `body` changes, not losses. The kept model files include those only the board names, and `body` and `model` losses give `convert.lossy` warnings, which `import --to python` keeps; the import holds every model the conversion writes. An authored PCB document with every body type (`tests/_altium_models.py`) converts with an empty `body` and `model` ledger, and KiCad puts every vertex at the source's pose.
- Close the remaining Altium import residue so that an Altium board converts without unreported loss (c0420). A Polygon Connect Style scope `IsNamedPolygon('n')` now governs the pour of that name (S-1790), so it no longer refuses the board's connect styles. A pad gets its own connection only when its records give it one style for every zone of its net, and keeps its setting with a `pad` change when they differ (B5). `Not InAnyNet` maps to the items without a net, and the Altium writer writes `NO_NET` back as `Not InAnyNet` (B7, S-1680). A converted zone carries the smallest Altium clearance that governs its polygon, with a `zone` change when that differs from its own; the DRU rules carry every wider value. KiCad 10.0.6 lets a matching custom rule decide a fill's gap and otherwise takes the larger of the zone's and the netclass clearance (B6, S-1792). The rule area of a keep-out of the Keep-Out layer forbids only what its source record's restrictions value forbids, so holes and pads inside a keep-out that allows them are no longer `items_not_allowed` (N2, N3, S-1711). A component with `COMPONENTKIND` 3 or 4 is a net tie (S-1791, S-0188): its pads form a net-tie group. The KiCad writer now writes `net_tie_pad_groups` on a created footprint instead of refusing it, and the Altium writer writes the component as `COMPONENTKIND` 4 (N1). `copper.hole-clearance` allows the clearance tolerance, as `copper.clearance` and KiCad's hole clearance test do (N4). Every board hole written as a generated KiCad footprint is now counted `changed`. `import --to python` of an Altium project keeps a part parameter that holds a TAB natively in the vendored footprint, as c0363 does for KiCad, so it no longer needs `--allow-lossy`. The level 7 pairing goes to c0421, and B8 and the footprint texts to c0422.
- Give every Altium rule record a fate in a conversion to KiCad (c0424, S-1830 to S-1838). The import now maps Silk To Silk Clearance, Component Clearance (courtyard), Differential Pairs Routing (gap and uncoupled length), Routing Layers (`disallow track` on the layers set `FALSE`; an internal plane has no key) and a Hole To Hole Clearance that allows stacked micro vias. A record the Altium writer cannot rebuild is kept on the board (`kept_rule`) and written back as it is, with the next priority of its kind. The conversion report has a third outcome, `kept` (`convert.kept`, info): a declared format limit for disabled records, the plane rules, Height, the silk-to-mask and silk-to-region clearances, the mask sliver, Net Antennae and the routing and test point kinds. A limit a KiCad constraint does not take, such as a via drill's `opt`, is left out and reported as changed: the drill's min and max now reach the DRU and `import --to python`. A Short Circuit rule of scope All that allows no short, and the first mask and paste expansions, are reported as changed. The Altium PCB writer accepts a key with an inner space (`TOP LAYER_V5`) in a kept rule record only.
- Keep everything an Altium footprint draws (c0422). `convert --to kicad` and `import --to python` write a component's own free texts as `fp_text user` children of its footprint, after its drawings and before its pads as KiCad 10.0.6 saves them, with their layer, size, stroke, justification and angle, mirrored on a back layer; the `footprint-text` row now counts them written (the public rows -02 to -08 lose none of their 644 texts). The footprint silkscreen and mechanical drawings that `equivalent` level 7 reports missing after a conversion are written (c0409); they come from the comparison, which c0421 completes across backends.
- Fix the two Altium edit operations that failed Part J case J23 (c0389 task 4.2, S-1588). `remap_layer` now selects a saved long track, arc or fill by its long layer id and moves that id with the layer byte; the census of the eight public documents (S-1654) shows that on Mechanical 17 to 32 the byte is 72 and only the long id names the layer. A flip moves the long id too. `set_rule` changes a Clearance's `GAP` and `GENERICCLEARANCE` together, because every public Clearance record holds them equal and Altium shows `GENERICCLEARANCE`. It refuses a rule whose two keys differ. Task 4.2 stays open for the J23–J33 rerun.
- Record the one-run Altium kit rerun after c0389 task 2.7 (S-1608, agent run in AD 24.5.2.23). Steps K1.1 to K1.8, K2.1 to K2.5, K3.1, K3.2, K5.1 and K6.1 pass in run `2026-11-05-074713b5` on the current kit, and `kit record` restores `H-A-WRITE-PCBDOC`, `H-A-PH-NO-CACHE`, `H-A-KIT-RESAVE`, `H-A-KIT-COMPILE` and `H-A-KIT-SCRIPT` to `ALTIUM-VERIFIED(kit; AD 24.5)`. The Altium graduation snapshot and the evidence matrix are regenerated.
- Record the Altium reruns after c0389 task 2.7 (S-1607, agent run in AD 24.5.2.23). C6 on the new `p0`: a split plane on Internal Plane 1 (`GND`) and the plane rules. The design rule check reports no Starved Thermal and no Un-Routed Net. Kit steps K2.4 and K5.1 to K5.5 on `board6` pass (run `2026-11-05-ddbe216c`), and `kit record` restores `H-A-PCBX-STACK`, `-VIASPAN`, `-TEXT` and `-KEEPOUT`. `H-A-WRITE-PCBDOC`, `H-A-PH-NO-CACHE`, `H-A-KIT-RESAVE`, `H-A-KIT-COMPILE` and `H-A-KIT-SCRIPT` stay `INFERRED` until the other samples' steps run on the current kit.
- Decide Draftsman gate G2 after round FPC (c0388 task 1.1): route P. The four root members named by the drop series let the minimal document open, but Import Changes leaves a written view empty (FPC2, FPC10) and the template route gives no board view (FPC3), so `draftsman.NATIVE_WRITE` stays `False`. With the gate closed, `build --target altium --altium-draftsman on` now writes the non-empty title-block fields as `[Parameter<n>]` sections into a project file that it writes, with no document and no other file changed. The info `altium.draftsman-not-written` names route P, and `docs/altium.md` gives the procedure for a document made from an Altium template. Tasks 3.3 and 6.1 to 6.3 and the requirement "Draftsman scope round trip" are cut. `H-A-DWF-MINIMAL`, `-IMPORT` and `-TEMPLATE` record the round and keep their levels.
- Record Draftsman round FPC (c0388 task 1.1, S-1606, agent run in AD 24.5.2.23). FPC1, FPC5, FPC6 and FPC10 open with no message. FPC2 opens, but Import Changes leaves its view empty. FPC3, used as a registered template, shows an `Object reference not set` error and gives no board view. FPC4 and the writer's probes FPC7, FPC8, FPC9 and FPC11 do not open. FPC10's BOM lists no row, and its saved copy rewrites the BOM's `ViewSide` to `All` and fills `BoardSourceName`. Of the drop series, only the probes without `ArrowStyles`, `DocumentOptions`, `FillStyles` or `LineStyles` are refused. By the round's rule G2 does not pass. No level moves.
- Correct the track and arc long-form row of `docs/formats/altium/pcb-copper.md`: the long layer id sits at offset 41 in a 45- or 49-byte track and 52 in a 56- or 60-byte arc, after five bytes, not after a solder-mask and a paste-mask expansion (census of the eight public documents, c0407 follow-up).
- Record Altium kit Part H (c0416 task 3.2, S-1605, agent run in AD 24.5.2.23). Altium reads a slot shorter than its hole as KiCad 10.0.6 does: the long side lies across the slot rotation (H2 axes 0°, 90°, 30°, 120°, 135° and 120°, 2.15 × 1 mm). NC Drill writes six plated routed slots along the same axes (H3). Save As keeps every pad's four numbers (H4, H6 readbacks pass), and the panel accepts a Length below the Hole Size (H5). `norm.slot_form` needs no change; `H-A-SLOT-SHORT` keeps its level.
- Fix Altium keep-out restrictions of tracks, arcs and fills (c0407 follow-up). A keep-out track, arc or fill of the long form holds its restrictions in the byte after its long layer id, as KiCad 10.0.6's import reads it; Fenolite gave every such keep-out all restrictions. The keep-out fills under the connectors of public row `-08` forbid pads alone (24) or nothing (0), so the copper check no longer reports 343 `copper.keepout` findings for their tracks and vias. A footprint's rule area that restricts nothing is written as a keep-out of value 0 instead of being refused (S-1711).
- Altium PCB documents with an internal plane now give the plane its net region and its rules (c0389 task 2.7, follow-up of case C6): each plane gets one `Split Plane` polygon after the pours, on its net, over the board outline, and `Rules6` ends with the `All` rules `PlaneConnect` (pads Relief 20/4/10/10 mil, vias Direct) and `PlaneClearance` (20 mil), the values of an unedited saved document (S-1653). C6 had reported Starved Thermal on the `GND` via and `D1` pad 1 and an Un-Routed Net between them; under the public rule definitions (S-1652) the sample's own holes starve neither thermal, so the missing net region is the likely cause. KiCad 10.0.6 now imports each plane's net as an unfilled zone over the outline on the plane's layer (the copper oracles say so). The variant `p0` (`4082a630…`) and the golden `board6.PcbDoc` change bytes, and `p0` needs a C6 rerun; documents without a plane keep their bytes. The kit sample `board6` holds a plane, so the kit runs `2026-11-05-e28d0bb1` and `-c91de60a` are stale for its steps K2.4 and K5.1–K5.5: nine register rows (`H-A-WRITE-PCBDOC`, `H-A-PH-NO-CACHE`, `H-A-KIT-RESAVE`, `H-A-KIT-COMPILE`, `H-A-KIT-SCRIPT`, `H-A-PCBX-STACK`, `-VIASPAN`, `-TEXT`, `-KEEPOUT`) go back to their level before the kit runs until those steps run again. `pcb-copper.md` records the split plane, the plane rule keys and the plane values that saved via and pad records cache, and corrects the order of the via's relief conductor width (32) and air gap (38).
- The library gallery takes each item's attribution and licence from its `PROVENANCE.json` (c0418): only an `authored` item says "Authored for fenolite-library"; a `seeded` item, such as the LibrePCB Base resistors, now says "Derived from <authors> (<upstream link>) at commit <commit>, licence <SPDX>; converted to Fenolite's formats, changes in its PROVENANCE.json" and links its upstream as its source; converted items keep their line. A provenance the gallery cannot attribute (unknown or missing origin, an authored item with an upstream, a third-party item without authors, an https link or a commit, or a licence that disagrees with the release index) makes `library gallery` exit 5 with `library.provenance-invalid` and write nothing; it never falls back to "Authored" or CC0.
- Record Altium kit Part Z (c0387 task 1.2, S-1604, agent run in AD 24.5.2.23). Board Side Bottom saves `BODYPROJECTION=1` and draws the body below the board. Place offers `Extruded 3D Body` and `3D Body`, and the panel's Model Type offers Generic, Extruded, Cylinder and Sphere (`MODELTYPE` 0 to 3). The panel keeps a negative standoff and swaps a standoff entered above the overall height. Case Z4: four generic bodies at Z 0/90/180/270 save four `Models` entries, each with its body's `ROTZ`, and Altium asks nothing about embedding (c0175 10.4 stays open on c0241's gates). The facts are `INFERRED` in `pcb-bodies.md`, and no level moves.
- Close four gaps of the KiCad to Altium write found by a private board rebuild (c0412). B12: a copper shape on a signal layer (filled polygon or rectangle, drawn line, arc, circle or outline) is written as a region, or as tracks and arcs, on its layer with its net, instead of a `copper-shape` loss; shapes on a plane, filled circles and shapes of an unwritten net stay a reported loss, and no fill record is written (its bytes after offset 37 are not documented). B14: clearance and other exact rules scoped by a reference, an item kind (track, via, pad, zone), `or` and `not` are written as `InComponent`, `IsTrack`, `IsVia`, `IsPad`, `InPolygon`, `Or` and `Not` queries, nested operators in parentheses; the reader takes `InPolygon`/`InPoly` as `item_kind zone` (S-1702); rules whose limits a record cannot hold exactly stay `value-unsupported`. B13: a `User.N` layer whose user name is `Mech.<n>` or `Mechanical <n>` is written on Mechanical n. B15: `build --target altium` of an imported design honours `footprint_definition` and writes the pinned definition, with `altium.footprint-definition` where its name differs from the native one, instead of refusing with `FEN-3004`.
- Library symbols place and hide their fields (c0419). A pins record's `[symbol]` may hold `fields`, a list of `{ name, at, rotation, justify, hide }` for `Reference`, `Value`, `Footprint`, `Datasheet` and `Description`; it gives `SymbolDef.fields` (new model value object `SymbolField`), and the KiCad symbol writer writes each placed field at its place with its justify and `(hide yes)`. A field that is not placed, and every symbol without `fields`, keeps today's bytes. Fix: a `line` graphic of more than two points was written with its first two points only (the rest of the drawing was lost without a finding); it is now one open polyline of all its points, and two-point lines keep their bytes. KiCad 10.0.6 saves both as written (`sym upgrade`).
- Propose c0417, the JLC supplier lookup that c0267 left out (maintainer decision 2026-11-04): proposal, design, spec delta and tasks only, no code. It stays blocked until the maintainer supplies the approved JLC API documentation and terms (task 0.1, the maintainer's); until then JLC remains unregistered.
- Altium import: 3D bodies of model type cylinder (2) and sphere (3) now have known heights, from standoff to overall height, as kit case Z2 saved them (c0387 task 2.1, S-1604); their volume is the box of the record's outline around the shape. A top part's body projected from the bottom (`BODYPROJECTION=1`) stays `altium.import.body-unknown`: Z1 shows it under the board, but not how far.
- The Draftsman correction kit (c0388 round FPC) gains FPC7 to FPC11: the writer's fabrication sheet in the corrected document, single-finding variants (named `BoardSourceName`, decimal note ids, dimensions alone) and a bottom BOM, with `expected.md` asking the writer's five open questions. The board's dimensions now snap in the PcbDoc's frame (outline lower-left at 1000 mil), as round F0's saved dimension shows, not the model's.
- The candidate Draftsman writer holds the Drill Table, the Layer Stack Legend, the BOM table, the Note and the new `LinearDimension` kind with every member of their round F0 rows and Altium's saved values (c0388 tasks 4.1 and 4.2, "Round F0 member values" in `docs/formats/altium/draftsman.md`); `draftsman_spec` places them, with the board's width and height as dimensions when `[fab].dimensions` is on. By the coordinator's amendment of 2026-11-05 this is written behind the closed gate: `draftsman.NATIVE_WRITE` stays `False`, so no command writes it; unit tests judge it against the F0 rows, and round FPC may force rework. The board Table is cut (c0172 cut order 4) and stays Texts.
- Footprints hold rule areas (c0411): `FootprintDef.keepouts` and `FootprintInstance.keepouts`, in the footprint's frame and the instance's pad frame. The Altium import makes them of a library footprint's keep-outs on copper and polygon cut-outs (c0410's loss `footprint-keepouts` is gone) and keeps a component's keep-outs in its footprint instead of moving them to the board; a footprint's keep-out region on the Keep-Out layer is an area on every copper layer, no longer an `Altium.KeepOut` graphic (one in public row `-04`); the PcbLib and PcbDoc writers write them as keep-out regions of the footprint or component. KiCad footprint zones with `keepout` are read as rule areas and written back, from the model for a created footprint; a board holds a footprint's zone points in its own frame, as KiCad 10.0.6 does, so a moved footprint moves them, and the library tree a KiCad build embeds now places them (before, they stayed at library coordinates). Placing a definition carries its areas, and the checks, pours, router and exports see every instance's areas through `model.board.rule_areas`; an area that forbids footprints does not judge its own footprint. Level 7 of `equivalent` compares a footprint's areas per footprint. `convert` makes the Keep-Out layer drawings of a footprint rule areas of that footprint.
- Footprint rule areas no longer stop the Altium writers (c0411 follow-up, lote-23 CI). A footprint area keeps the board's copper layers it names, and an area on all of them is one Keep-Out layer region. A copper layer the board lacks is left out. A non-copper layer, such as `BT1`'s `B.CrtYd` on public row `-06`, is counted changed under `keep-out`, and an area with no copper layer is counted lost. The PcbLib puts an area on `In<n>.Cu` on Mid-Layer n. Written areas now count under `keep-out`. A KiCad footprint file that keeps a board footprint's `at` reads its zones in the footprint's frame, so the footprint round trip of public row `-13` holds. With c0412, rows `-11` and `-14` no longer lose `copper-shape` items.
- Altium kit Part H (c0416 task 3.1) asks what Altium Designer makes of a slot whose length is below its hole size: six slot pads on a free board and in a free library, cases H1 to H6, built outside the repository by `tests/unit/backends/altium/test_part_h.py`, which also reads back the files Altium saves. `H-A-SLOT-SHORT` is registered as `INFERRED`, and Part H has not been run.
- Level 3 of `equivalent` compares a slot's length and axis (c0416), with the new difference `pad-slot` (issue code `equiv.pad-slot`). A slot is read by its extents, as KiCad's oval is: the longer of `Pad.drill` and `Padstack.hole_length` is its length, and its axis, in the footprint's frame and modulo a half turn, lies along that side. So an Altium slot whose length is below its hole size equals KiCad's oval of it, while a longer slot, a slot along the other axis or a round hole of the slot's width is reported. `pcb-records.md` records how `fp upgrade` 10.0.6 writes slot pads.
- Fix the wired schematic default of c0406 after the full suite (c0406): a power flag is added only for a declared supply or a supply net without a part's own power input, so KiCad's ERC still reports an undriven power input as with the readable layout; a flag on a root sheet that holds none of its net's pins carries a global label instead of a power symbol, which KiCad reported unconnected; a sheet with a symbol pin off the 1.27 mm grid falls back to readable (`build.schematic-wired-fallback`). The byte pins of the blink builds were recorded again (schematic, flag library, `build.json` only).
- Library review acceptances now pin the reviewed content, not the viewer (c0415). `library review --accept` writes `content_sha256` into `[[reviewed_by]]`: the SHA-256 of what the sheet shows (its embedded scene data, unpacked, and its record id, metadata, findings and visual-diff payloads), without `page.html`, `viewer.css`, `viewer.js` or the scene packing. A review with `content_sha256` goes stale (`library.review-stale`) when the record digest or that content digest changes, so a Fenolite upgrade that changes only the viewer no longer invalidates accepted records. Reviews written before c0415 keep their rule (record digest and sheet bytes) and still verify; `library review TARGET... --pin-content` adds the content digest to such a review in place while its sheet bytes still match, and refuses a stale one with `FEN-4002`; `--viewer DIR` builds the sheet with the viewer files of the Fenolite that wrote the review, for reviews written by an earlier version. `library check` rows give `sheet.content_sha256`, and `sheets.json` items give `content_sha256`.
- Altium keep-outs, c0407's follow-ups (c0410). The rule areas that `convert` makes of Keep-Out layer drawings now forbid pads too, as KiCad 10.0.6's `pcb import` does. A keep-out on copper or a polygon cut-out in a PcbLib footprint no longer becomes copper of the footprint. It is a reported loss: census category `footprint-keepouts`, and the hint of `altium.import.unmapped` names c0411, which will give footprint definitions rule areas. `pcb-records.md` records what `fp upgrade` 10.0.6 makes of a library's keep-outs and what `pcb import` makes of the older unflagged Keep-Out drawings (S-1710).
- Fix Altium keep-outs and polygon cut-outs (c0407). A region of `KIND=1` (a polygon cut-out) is now a rule area that forbids pours, on its copper layer or on every copper layer from the Keep-Out or Multi-Layer layer; it was filled copper without a net on a copper layer, a fabrication error (B2). A track, arc, fill or region with the keep-out flag on a copper layer, free or of a component, is a rule area of its band or outline on that layer, not copper (B4). `convert` makes each keep-out track, arc or circle of the Keep-Out layer the rule area of the band its width covers, as KiCad 10.0.6's `pcb import` does, instead of filling the ring the tracks enclose; only a filled shape is one area, and a width-0 line is a loss (B1). Level 3 of `equivalent` compares a slot's width with a width (`pad-drill`), so an Altium slot whose length is below its hole size no longer differs from KiCad's oval of the same sizes (B3). Imported Altium boards now carry these rule areas, and the public rows `-01` and `-06` lose no keep-out graphic.
- `fenolite panel --target altium` now writes Altium's Embedded Board Array (c0389 tasks 3.3 and 3.4): `pcbdoc.EMBEDDED_ARRAYS` is `True`. By the coordinator's decision of 2026-11-05, given by the maintainer's delegation, a restart counts as Q8's reopen and Q9 is judged with the separate drill files and the `V-Cut` Gerber on (neither is stored in the PCB document), so Q7, Q8 and Q9 of Part Q are as expected in Altium 24.5.2.23. A panel of a `.PcbDoc` board with an unturned, unmirrored grid is written as one array that names the board by its path relative to the output folder, with no copy of the board; `result.panel.form` is `embedded` and the reply's evidence names `H-A-PANEL-RECORD`, `H-A-PANEL-LAYERKIND` and `H-A-PANEL-KIT`. A `.kicad_pcb` board, a turned or mirrored grid or a board with no relative path gives the flattened panel with `altium.panel-flattened`, and `--altium-panel embedded` is refused for them (`FEN-7001`, exit 7). Limitation: Altium rereads a changed board only after it is restarted. The three rows keep their levels (agent run); the C6 plane failure of task 2.4 is a new open task, 2.7.
- Record the K2 rerun of the Altium kit with the fixed script (c0389 task 6.1, S-1603, agent run in AD 24.5.2.23). The script creates `results/script.log` and appends `K2.1 done` to `K2.5 done`, `kit verify` passes K2.1 to K2.5, and run `2026-11-05-c91de60a` is recorded; `H-A-KIT-SCRIPT` is `ALTIUM-VERIFIED(kit; AD 24.5; 2026-11-05; 2026-11-05-c91de60a)`. Part R steps R1 and R2 in AD 24.5.2.23 Trial (c0389 task 2.5, S-1602): the `Repeat` sheet does not expand ("Missing child-sheet in two_ch.SchDoc in Symbol Repeat(CH,1,2)"), also through the UI. This is an observation scoped to that version; the AD 26.5 pass of 2026-10-14 stands and no writer or level changes.
- c0384 records the verify-source reruns on 41cf1ed8 (closure checks and the shared-name exemption): the public RoyalBlue54L-Feather and flat_hierarchy demos pass all nine levels under "L7 after identity and order preparation" (RoyalBlue 1522 identities rewritten, 1125 moved; flat_hierarchy 625 and 378), and task 1.4 points to c0408 for the build's net spelling.
- Fix: a built board now carries KiCad's net names where the schematic built beside it names them (c0408, from c0384 finding 1.4): a pin on no net whose name equals its pin number is named like a pin without a name (`unconnected-(J1-Pad1)`, S-1490), and a net that a drawn schematic names by a local or hierarchical label of a child sheet keeps the raw slashes of the sheet path (`/pic_programmer/VPP_ON`, not `{slash}pic_programmer{slash}VPP_ON`). `lens.build.sheet_spellings` reads the written sheets and keeps the spelling as the net's `stored` pair; the schematic generator is unchanged. The public flat_hierarchy build failed KiCad's parity test against its own sheets in 67 pads; an authored case of that shape now passes it on 9.0.9 and 10.0.6.
- Add: `fenolite render --page auto|sheet|board` (c0409, B11). The SVG plots used to be drawn on the board's drawing page, so a board outside it, such as one at an Altium origin with negative Y, came out blank. `auto`, the default, now plots the board area alone (`--exclude-drawing-sheet --page-size-mode 2`) when the outline reaches outside the page; `result.svg_page` names the page used. The optional key refreshes the `fenolite.render.v0` schema hash in `schemas/frozen-v0.json`.
- Fix: an Altium conversion carries the project parameters (c0409, B10). The `[Parameter<n>]` entries of a `.PrjPcb` become text variables of the board and the project, and a board text that is the special string `.NAME` of one, or holds it as `'.NAME'`, is written as `${NAME}`. A parameter whose name is no variable name gives `altium.import.unmapped`; any other special string is written as typed, with a `text` change in the report.
- Add: `select.no_net()`, the items without a net (c0409, B7). An Altium rule scoped `InNet('No Net')` now imports as a rule on it instead of being unmapped, and KiCad gets the condition `A.NetName == ''`. The model selector is `not(net "*")`, built from the frozen ops of model v0.
- Fix: an Altium conversion keeps the pour settings and the 3D models (c0409, B5, B6, B9). Each zone now takes the connection, thermal gap and spoke width of the Polygon Connect Style rule that governs the pads of its net, and a pad whose narrower rule joins it otherwise gets its own zone connection; a zone's clearance is the Altium clearance rule that governs the polygon against any item, not the 0.5 mm default. The board's footprints now hold the placed models, so `import --to python` writes the same `(model` entries as `convert --to kicad`, and it writes the model files at the paths they name.
- Fix: an imported rule keeps the source's rank (c0409, B4). The importer used to fold a one-sided rule "<class> x All" into `design.rules.minimum(netclass=)`, which governs every rule of its class (c0403), so it overrode the pairwise rules Altium ranks above it, and it numbered every `rule()` by its place in the `.kicad_dru`. A rule now becomes a minimum only where the minimum keeps its rank; any other one-sided rule is `rule(where=select.netclass(...))` with its priority, and a rule that Fenolite named `fenolite_<p>_<name>` gets its number `p` back (Altium's own) when those numbers give the file's order.
- Fix: an Altium import keeps each footprint's own drawing (c0409, B8). `convert --to kicad` and `import --to python` now write a footprint's lines, arcs, circles and regions as `fp_*` children of the board and library footprints, on the layer the conversion maps (an Altium mechanical layer to the `User.<n>` layer named after it). A footprint's own free text, which a created footprint does not write yet, is a reported `footprint-text` loss, and losses of `footprint-graphic` or `footprint-text` give a `convert.lossy` warning without refusing, so an import says so too.
- An empty schematic `Footprint` field beside a board footprint stays empty through import and build (c0414): `schematic.toml` marks that symbol unit `empty_footprint = true` (a flag, never a text), the KiCad drawn writer writes the field empty in its pose and font, and the part keeps its board footprint. A field that names another footprint still refuses (c0342). With c0413, the public demo `kicad-demo-9-0-9-1-pcb-01` now round-trips equal at all nine levels on KiCad 10.0.6 and becomes a measured fidelity row.
- The derived teardrop proof now qualifies rotated and custom pad parents (c0413): a footprint or pad at any whole-microdegree angle is turned with the fixed-point sine and cosine and an explicit error bound, and a contact the bound cannot settle refuses; a custom pad with `(clearance convexhull)` qualifies, and its body grows from the anchor to the primitive copper known from inside (line strokes with round caps, filled circles, and filled polygons or rectangles under quarter-turn poses). Authored contact controls agree with KiCad DRC on 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 (S-1740). On the public demo `kicad-demo-9-0-9-1-pcb-01` the proof now regenerates six equal caches on each side with KiCad 10.0.6, so its teardrop `opaque-count` is gone.
- Fix the Fenolite bugs found by a private board rebuild (c0403): the Specctra export lowers clearance rules in KiCad's rule order, so a higher-priority lower pair rule and a lower rule between the nets of one class reach the router, with `specctra.rule-widened` when a value is kept higher (F8); the copper check, the build's guards and `check` judge copper against drilled holes with `copper.hole-clearance` (F9); the build refuses script copper narrower than its governing `track_width` rule with `copper.track-width` (F10), and `route` refuses router copper below it with `route.width-below-minimum` (F6); plans are counted per working folder, 128 in all (F7); the `minimum()` docstring and the page `board-insulation` state that a class minimum governs the rules of its class, and how to scope an exception inside one part (F2–F4); a regression test covers the zero-size pad crash fixed by c0364 (F5). F11 and library items go to their owners. Builds and routes that held such copper now report an error and plan no write.
- Fix: `fenolite blocks capture` now gives copper graphics read from the board the recorded id of their net, as it does for tracks, arcs, vias and zones. `tools/model_review.py --check` compares the reviewed model shape (classes, fields, types, defaults, extension keys, decisions) and only notes moved reader locations, and a unit test runs it against the stored inventory, which is written again (c0404).
- c0279 task 4.4 is done: the library's gallery deploys GitHub Pages (https://lgili.github.io/fenolite-library/) from its `main` without a GitHub Release, rebuilding the `v1.0.0` assets from the tag (library PR 1, runs 42004334439 and 42005521750); pic_programmer's page names its render failure until task 4.6 reaches the library pin.
- A footprint placed at an angle keeps its `point` children without an angle: KiCad 10.0.6 refuses to load a board holding `(point (at x y θ))`, which stopped the library gallery from rendering the pic_programmer board (c0279 task 4.6).
- An Altium-to-KiCad conversion now places the copies of an Embedded Board Array where Altium puts them (S-1592) for an unmirrored array turned by 0 or 90 degrees whose referenced board has its origin at its shape's lower-left corner: each copy's components, copper and holes join the board, designators suffixed `_B<k>`, nets prefixed `B<k>/`, parts in a generated sheet `B<k>`; the array is reported `changed`. A mirrored array, another angle, an origin away from that corner, board cut-outs or extra copper layers stay a refused `embedded-board` loss with their own reason. The panel writer and the conversion share the copy step (`model.replicate`) (c0386, task 2.2).
- An Altium-to-KiCad conversion now reads the PCB document that each Embedded Board Array references, at its path relative to the board's document, and the report's reason names a document that is missing, does not read, or is stored as an absolute path (named by its file name only; not followed). Every array is still a refused `embedded-board` loss: the copies' places are not measured yet (c0386, task 2.1).
- Part Z of the Altium kit, component bodies (c0387, task 1.1): cases Z1 to Z3 ask Altium for a body on the other side of its component, each 3D body type the UI offers, and heights at the limits the Properties panel accepts; case Z4 places one model at four rotations (c0175, task 10.4). The kit uses the committed `body2` project and the authored CC0 `Box_2x1.step`; `test_part_z.py -k saved` prints the bodies and models of the saved files after the kit privacy scan. No import rule or level changes before the run.
- Draftsman correction round FPC (c0388, task 1.1): the Part F kit test builds FP3 and FP4 again with the 32 root members of the empty F0.1 control that they lack, a template probe for Preferences, a probe with only the three empty style lists, the control with the writer's sheet, a format version 24 variant, and a drop series that takes one root member at a time out of the control. The probes are built from the person's own reviewed F0 folder and stay outside the repository. A new format row records the order of a document's root members (`Id` first, then by name). - Fix: the Altium kit script creates `results/script.log` (c0389, task 6.1). `FileExists` on the log's path now selects `Rewrite` when the file is missing and `Append` otherwise. Altium Designer 24.5.2.23 had stopped the script on the `EInOutError` of `Append` on the missing log, even inside `Try`-`Except` (run `2026-11-05-e28d0bb1`, S-1590). `FileExists` is listed in `SCRIPT_CALLS` with its public source (S-1591).
