`build.image-missing`, `build.image-format`), the model holds an `Image` with the base64 data (`Board.images`), the KiCad writer and reader handle the root `image`, an Altium build reports it (`altium.image-skipped`), and the import writes `design.image()` with the PNG beside the code. `Footprint.pad()` takes shaped pads: `shape="trapezoid"` with `delta`, `chamfer` with `corners`, `radius_ratio`, and `shape="custom"` with `anchor` and `primitives` made by `poly()`, `segment()`, `circle()` and `arc()` of `fenolite.dsl.footprint` (`Pad.trapezoid_delta`, `chamfer_ratio`, `chamfer_corners`, `anchor_shape`, `primitives`, which a KiCad reader projects from a read pad). `Part.field()` takes `bold`, `italic` and `face` (`FootprintField.bold`, `italic`, `face`), written into the field's font. `fenolite verify-source` builds with the copper guard warning instead of refusing, and level 6 retains the integrated comparison by layer name, kind and relative order instead of comparing shifted ordinals. `Part.override()` sets the footprint-level clearance, margins, paste ratio, zone connection and thermal relief of one instance (`FootprintInstance.overrides`), and `Part.edit_pad()` changes the pads of one instance (`build.pad-unknown` for a number the footprint lacks). `Part.model(name, offset=, rotation=, scale=, hidden=)` names the 3D models of a part's footprint instance (`FootprintInstance.models`, `ModelRef`), written in place of the footprint's own; the name is never read. Current import-emission observations now cover all 18 declared demo boards with input and measurement hashes, while historical full-conversion evidence stays unchanged and CI still builds and checks equivalence independently. Refresh the entry inventory/source notes and prove the unchanged byte baseline while keeping the full and native gates open.

- **Net roles, phase 4: route proposals** (c0280): `fenolite route --router octilinear --propose` lists every
  open connection left after the power copper and the critical routes with candidate shapes and their facts,
  without a score; `--accept ID` locks a candidate through a reviewed plan, `--reject ID` remembers a refusal,
  `--replay` restores locked decisions (c0280). Subjects (`fanout`, `join`, `role-net`, `group`, `connection`,
  `fenolite.roles.proposals`) come from the open connections on the pour, taken along a role net's power pads,
  and from the groups phase 2 leaves open; the octilinear router answers `RoutingJob.subjects` with
  `RoutingResult.candidates` (feature `propose`; families `shape`, `z-shape`, `via-l`, `fanout`, `role` with one
  relaxation at a time). Each candidate's facts: connections closed, open connections before and after, new
  copper-check and role findings, vias, length, layers, bends, loop, clashes, fill and what was not measured;
  with `--gate drc`, KiCad's DRC in batches. Decisions live in `fenolite.route-decisions.v0`
  (`.fenolite/route-decisions.json`); `--forget ID` drops one. Eight `route.proposal-*` codes. A role-mode entry
  left open names `propose`. `fenolite view --proposals run|FILE` joins a reply to the board
  (`fenolite.view_proposals.v0`), a served page may run `proposals` and plan `route-accept` and
  `route-reject`. On the six role-copper reference variants the accepted proposals leave no open connection.
- **Net roles in the viewer** (c0305; the role view of c0245-engineering-viewer, brought into this viewer by
  the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-29): `fenolite view BOARD --roles run` runs `fenolite roles BOARD
  --check` read-only (or `--roles FILE` reads its reply) and joins it to the board as `scene.roles`
  (`fenolite.view_roles.v0`, `fenolite.view.roles`); `result.roles` counts roles and findings, and the reply's
  evidence is the lower of the board's and the roles'. A Net roles panel lists a legend of the role kinds with
  colours and glyphs, each net's role, the hot loops, gate loops and Kelvin pairs and the role findings; the
  Layers tab gets role colours of the copper, hatched loops with their measured area, dashed Kelvin sense lines
  and role markers; the inspector shows a Role section after a net's Net section. `check_overlay` places
  `roles.*` findings (stage `roles.rules`) on the group or `group:pad` they name. A served page runs `roles`
  (`roles --check`, no option) and, with `--allow-actions`, plans Confirm roles (`roles --accept`,
  `--accept-group`, only names its role data shows as inferred) through the plan, confirm and undo of c0303.
- **Checks run from the viewer** (c0302): a page served by `fenolite view --serve` can run `check`, `analyze`, `ready` and `equivalent` itself, read-only, one job at a time. Each Project review card whose stages the server can run gets a **Run** control, and **Run from this page** picks the stages, the analyze kinds, a readiness check or a comparison with another document. A strip shows the command, its progress, the elapsed time and **Cancel**; a cancelled or timed-out run changes nothing. A `check` run replaces, stage by stage, the stages of the same name (`fenolite.view.project_review.merge`), each stage keeping its own input hash and evidence, so a stage of an older revision reads Limited; `analyze` paths become finding groups, `ready` and `equivalent` findings join the review list with Locate. Server routes `GET /runs`, `POST /run`, `GET /run/<job>`, `POST /run/<job>/cancel` behind the guard of `POST /marks` (`Host`, `Origin`, `X-Fenolite-Run: 1`, JSON, 16 KiB); the allow-list `fenolite.view.runs` takes a command name, a document id and typed options, never a command line or a path. Each run is a child process through the runner core shared with `fenolite mcp`, now `fenolite.cli.childrun`. New options `--run-timeout SECONDS` (default 600) and `--no-run`; `result.serve.runs` counts the runs by final state. `docs/viewer.md`, "Running checks from the page".
- **Writing actions from the viewer** (c0303): `fenolite view --serve --allow-actions` lets the page fill zones, tidy copper (with `--gate drc`), place designators or clip silkscreen, place test points, waive a KiCad DRC finding with a reason (`check --exclude`), move or rotate a part (`place --move`), shift or turn parts with their copper, and export fabrication files or the BOM under the project. Each action is a dry run first: a plan dialog lists the files (sizes, new and replaced SHA-256), the issues and the evidence, and draws the staged board's changes over the current one with the revision compare; Confirm writes exactly that plan (`--confirm --plan`), a changed file asks for a new review (`FEN-4002`), and the receipt follows, kept in `view-receipts/` of the state folder so that Undo plans `fenolite restore` on it. Routes `GET /actions`, `POST /action/plan`, `/action/confirm`, `/action/undo`, behind the run guard with `X-Fenolite-Action: 1` and a per-session token; the allow-list `fenolite.view.actions` fills fixed templates with typed options, confines outputs to the project root and writes no Altium document. `result.serve.writes` lists the session's receipts. `docs/viewer.md`, "Actions: writing from the page".
- **A professional shell for the viewer** (c0300): one set of interface tokens (spacing, type, radii,
  surfaces, text, accent, focus, severity) with a dark and a light theme that follows the system until the user
  picks one (`fenolite.view.theme`), each text colour at 4.5:1 or more on its surface; a document keeps its own
  colours. The toolbar is grouped as View (2D/3D, Fit, Top/Bottom with the far side's opacity), Tools
  (Measure with Point and Edge gap, Mark, mm/mil) and Share (Copy link, Export: SVG and Print at a scale), each
  button with an icon drawn for Fenolite, an accessible name and a tooltip with its shortcut; the duplicate
  "Fit" and "Clear" controls are gone. One command registry (`viewer/src/core/commands.ts`) feeds the toolbar,
  the shortcuts, the shortcut sheet (`?`) and a command palette (`Ctrl+K`, `Cmd+K` on macOS) that also jumps to
  parts, nets, documents and library items (`>` commands, `@` parts, `#` nets). The side bar opens with the
  Project review and its card states, then one remembered disclosure per panel with a count; Components and
  Nets sort (reference in natural order, value, footprint; name, pads, routed length) and finding groups at 0
  fold into one line. The status bar shows the cursor, the grid, the zoom, the layer, the selection, the unit
  and the live state; the SHA-256, size, backend and evidence moved to "About this document". Loading shows a
  progress bar, a failed read offers Retry, empty lists say why. Run checks of c0302 is in the toolbar and the
  palette. The visual tests run in the dark interface theme, `kicad_board_light.png` covers the light one, and
  the references were regenerated and re-reviewed byte-identically with the exact CI browser. Documentation proof now names the actual tested control modules. `docs/viewer.md`, "The page".
- **The viewer's inspector, Layers panel and 3D controls** (c0301): a dock at the right of the drawing, never over
  it, with a Properties tab (the inspector) and, on boards and footprints, a Layers tab that the 3D panel replaces
  in 3D; `I` hides it, its edge resizes it (240 to 480 px), and at 760 px and below it is a bottom sheet that a
  selection opens and `Esc` closes. The inspector shows the selection in sections (Part, Pad, Track, Arc, Via,
  Zone, Net, Keep-out, Finding; sheets, libraries and drawings alike), each value in the page's unit with a copy
  control, "Copy all", values the file does not hold left out, and the document's summary when nothing is
  selected; its actions are registry commands also listed in the palette: Zoom to, Highlight net, Select part,
  Find similar, Show in schematic or Show on board, Open in review, and with `--allow-actions` Move part and
  Waive finding (c0303). A selected net, part or filter result is drawn in its own colours with an outline in the
  `--emphasis` token instead of being lightened, its zone fills lighter and outlined, the rest dimmed at 22 %.
  The Layers panel lists every layer by its full name in groups with visibility, opacity, the current layer and
  the presets, so the layer tabs leave the bottom bar of boards (sheets keep their document tabs). In 3D the
  2D-only controls are hidden, a view cube sets the six face views and the corner views (animated), the light is
  an ambient, a key and a fill term, extruded bodies take a charcoal body colour with a lighter top, and the 3D
  panel shows or hides bodies, board, silkscreen and copper. New visual tests (`test_inspector.py`,
  `test_layers.py`, `test_3d.py`) and the reference `kicad_board_net.png`; the references were regenerated with
  the dock. Their eleven-image local re-review changes no baseline; the final batch CI remains required. `docs/viewer.md`, "The page" and "Units and measurement".
- **Waive and stale results in the viewer** (c0303, two fixes found in testing): Waive is not offered on a
  finding of a check of an earlier revision of the file, and the page says why; after an action writes a project
  file the checks read (a waiver writes the `.kicad_pro`), the document's review results are marked stale until a
  check runs again, and the run strip, the Project review and the inspector offer Run again.
- **Fidelity corpus baselines** (c0278): ten `rtf` demo rows that round-trip with no difference at any level are measured
  zero-count rows of `tests/kicad/fidelity/accepted.toml`, so the corpus test runs them instead of skipping. The other 14
  stay pending with their 2026-11-04 outcome. The kicad-10 job fetches `--uses rtf`. `H-K-FID-ZONE-OUTLINE` names its
  10.0-only test. The 2026-11-04 census is in `docs/evidence/fidelity.md`.

### Changed

- Keep the all-example Altium lowering corpus strict for populated boards while checking the new mechanical Part S example against all four holes and every vertex of its three pinned authored cut-outs (c0168).

- Reconcile the completed V2/N4/C5 Viewer requests with their dated blocked processing outcomes; retain pending rendering hypotheses and distinguish the two-minute native-saved control (c0166).

- Make the Part S regions recipe an executable authored DSL Design, preserving the migration example corpus and all eleven pinned CAD inputs (c0168).

- **The viewer page is built from TypeScript modules** (c0304; the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-29, before
  the redesign of c0300 and c0301): the old single `src/fenolite/view/page.html` (188 KB of inline CSS, markup
  and vanilla JavaScript) is now about fifty modules under `viewer/src/` (`core`, `board`, `measure`, `three`,
  `sch`, `panels`, `dialogs`, `table`, `library`, `export`), type-checked by the TypeScript compiler and linked
  by `viewer/build.mjs` into one script whose top-level names stay page globals; the board, sheet and WebGL
  renderers are moved, not rewritten. Preact (MIT, bundled, listed in `NOTICE`) renders the Changes, Messages,
  Findings and Navigator panels. The build writes `src/fenolite/view/static/` (`page.html`, `viewer.js`,
  `viewer.css`, `manifest.json`), which is committed and inlined by `fenolite.view.export.template()`, so the
  served page and `--export` stay one offline file and installing Fenolite needs no Node. `make viewer`
  rebuilds (Node 22, `tools/build_viewer.py`), `make viewer-check` fails on a stale bundle and runs in the
  `viewer` CI job, and `tests/unit/view/test_viewer_bundle.py` checks the recorded digests without Node.
  Nothing changes on screen: the page's unit tests and the 69 visual tests with their references pass unchanged.
  The page code of c0302 and c0303 lives in `review/runs.ts`, `panels/run-strip.tsx` and the Run controls of
  `panels/project-review.ts`, and in `actions/actions.ts`, `dialogs/plan-review.tsx` and `panels/receipts.tsx`.
- **The viewer changes of v0.7 renumbered** (2026-10-29): written as c0243 to c0247 (and c0255 for
  `viewer-roles`), renumbered c0250 to c0255 (which freed c0243 to c0245 for another session's changes), then
  c0268 to c0273, they are now c0300 (`viewer-shell`), c0301 (`viewer-inspector`), c0302 (`viewer-run-checks`),
  c0303 (`viewer-actions`), c0304 (`viewer-build`) and c0305 (`viewer-roles`): on 2026-10-29 the range c0300 to
  c0305 was reserved for the viewer, away from the c0246 to c0279 of the maintainer's `circuits-as-code` line
  (which continues from c0280 in its own line). New changes on this branch start at c0306.

### Fixed

- Docs: source row S-2100 and the archived c0451 proposal and design name a generic special string (`.MY_TAG`) as their example.
- **Coincident tracks in strict level 5** (c0401): tracks, arcs or vias with the same key now match first
  with an item whose width, net and lock are equal, so two coincident tracks of different widths written
  in another order are no longer reported as two `track-width` differences. A changed width is still
  reported. This was the cause of `kicad-demo-10-0-6-pcb-14`'s four differences.
- **Rectangle board outlines** (c0401): an imported board whose outline is one root `gr_rect` on Edge.Cuts
  keeps it as `design.rect(..., layer="Edge.Cuts")`, and the build writes it back as one `gr_rect` instead
  of four `gr_line`. `design.rect` takes `Edge.Cuts` unfilled; a filled one is refused. This was the cause
  of `kicad-demo-9-0-9-1-pcb-05`'s five `graphic-missing` differences.
- **Exact copper arcs and bottom chamfers** (c0282): copper pairs, rule areas and curved board edges use exact circular distances instead of polygonisation bands, removing arc-band false clearance findings near limits while preserving the injected tolerance. Copper graphic arcs retain the same exact cores. Engineering analyses retain their bounded arc polylines. Bottom placement and derivation mirror chamfer corner names and retain their ratios; builds using this new mirror carry inferred evidence pending KiCad oracle confirmation.
- **KiCad-job tests after c0250, c0252, c0253 and c0269**: the expected `fenolite check` stage lists of `test_canary` and `test_built_blink_before_routing` hold `circuit.rules` right after `model.validate`; the device build oracle test and the `sensor_rails` read-back accept the info findings `circuit.rating-missing` (shipped devices carry no ratings) and `build.block-port-merged` and nothing else; `tools/catalog/golden.py` leaves out the files later changes added (`LATER`: the base record `fenolite-base.json`) and their entry in the `files` map of `.fenolite/build.json`, whose other bytes still equal the c0259 golden.
- **ERC fact fixture after c0247**: the `pin_not_driven` control of the ERC facts (`tests/kicad/check/_erccases.py`) no longer edits the blink's former `no_connect(...)` line, which c0247 replaced with `u1.unused_no_connect()`; it connects the two input pins and lets the build's unused-pin request leave them unmarked. The recorded probe outcomes do not change.
- **Topology length oracle** (c0258): `tests/kicad/rules/test_topology_lowering.py` looks for the
  `length_out_of_range` of the lowered `fenolite_0_topology_clk_delay` rule on any track or pad of the timed
  net. KiCad 10.0.6 names the net's two pads, not its track, so the over-budget case failed before.
- **Design lock path on Windows** (c0249): the build plan and its receipt name the design lock with forward slashes on every system (`ex/design.lock`, never `ex\design.lock`).
- **Acceptance rebuild test after c0250**: `test_rebuild_is_the_identity` expects the base record `fenolite-base.json` among the files a first build adds to the recorded v0.1 projects, which predate it; a second build is still the identity.

- **The viewer's Node-run tests on Windows**: the tests that run `node` (`tests/unit/view/`, `tests/_visual.py`)
  decode its output as UTF-8 instead of the locale's code page, so `×` and `²` no longer turn into cp1252
  garbage in `unit (windows-latest, py3.12)`.

## [0.6.0] - 2026-10-29

### Added

- **Net roles, phase 0** (c0222): Fenolite infers what each net of a power board does (`power`, `switch_node`, `ground`, `kelvin`, `gate`, `signal`, `high_voltage`) and its hot loops, gate loops and Kelvin pairs from the circuit, with a part class, a rule and a confidence for each, and judges the copper against them through the analyses of `dev` (`loop_areas`, `analyze_power`, `path_network`, `analyze_necks`, `analyze_split`, `analyze_distances`, `analyze_returns`, `analyze_coupling`, `analyze_ties`), defining no area, section, capacity or gap of its own. `fenolite roles PATH [--check]` shows and checks them, read-only until `--accept`, `--accept-all [--min-confidence]`, `--set NET=KIND[:KEY=N]`, `--reject`, `--accept-group`, `--reject-group` or `--current NET=AMPS` plans the role document `<board stem>.roles.json` (schema `fenolite.roles.v0`) or `.fenolite/rules.json` of a built project; `--check` takes the measuring options of `analyze` and runs no tool. The stage `roles.rules` of `fenolite check` (both pipelines, after `placement.rules`) runs the same judge on a board with stored or declared roles and is skipped with `no-roles` otherwise. A design script declares roles with `design.net_role`, `hot_loop`, `kelvin`, `gate_loop`, `role_limits` and `part_class`, stored as `declared` in `RuleSet.roles`; a rebuild keeps the confirmed ones. Seventeen `roles.*` codes (with `fenolite explain`) and the check code `check.roles-unreadable`; a finding against a default of the role profile (`fenolite.roles.profile`, each default with its source, `docs/roles.md`) is at most a warning, an error only against your limit on a confirmed or declared role. Three reference boards authored for Fenolite (`examples/roles/buck_shunt`, `half_bridge`, `bms`, good and bad) with CC0 parts in `tests/data/libs/Roles.*`; the good variants pass KiCad DRC and give no warning, the bad ones exactly their designed findings, on the KiCad and the Altium build. Six `H-G-ROLE-` hypotheses registered. The agent guide's generated pages may now hold 750 lines.
- **Release 0.6.0: the MCP server and the board engineering tools** (c0242): `fenolite mcp` and `fenolite edit` (c0195, c0196); the board engineering tools of c0175 in their own changes: router input fidelity, ground ties, barrier crossings, return paths, practice checks, pour shapes, region placement, layout reuse and group moves, copper tidy, DRC trials and `--gate drc`, diff gates, region renders, designators and silkscreen clipping, test-point placement, the octilinear router, generated copper, library fidelity, file safety and fabrication read-back (c0197 to c0214, c0223 to c0229); the Python API (c0206); computed Altium pours, experimental (c0215); the release from a tag (c0221); reliability, viewer review, the catalog browser and the public presentation (c0232 to c0240); and the hypotheses and kit Part J of the Altium edit sessions (c0241). The record `docs/release/v0.6.md` gives, change by change, what ships, its proof and its open tasks; the maintainer's tests (the Altium kit runs, the viewer reviews, a KiCad GUI save, the runs of a real agent) are deferred to v0.7, and the v1.0 freeze (c0216 to c0220) is held by his decision of 2026-10-29. Every Altium write kind stays experimental.
- **Altium edit sessions, measured first** (c0241, task 1.1; package 12 of c0175, moved on 2026-10-29): the hypotheses of the edit session (`H-A-EDIT-*`, fourteen rows: what Altium Designer changes in a document when it moves, turns, flips, deletes, copies, places, re-layers or re-rules one object, and what a plain save rewrites) and of the integrity rules (`H-A-INTEG-*`, seven rows: polygon sub-indices, pour indices, unique ids, parameter groups and orphans, the model store, two layer fields) are registered, each `INFERRED` with its kit case, before any write code. Kit Part J (`docs/evidence/altium-edit.md`): phase A, an authored two-layer board (`tests/data/altium_edit/edit.py`) and cases J1 to J22 that the maintainer edits once each and saves in Altium Designer, built outside the repository by `FENOLITE_ALTIUM_PART_J=<folder> uv run pytest tests/unit/backends/altium/test_part_j.py -k phase_a` with `expected.md`, the steps in Brazilian Portuguese and `SHA256SUMS`; phase B (J23 to J33) is described for the documents the session will write and for planted faults. No edit is written yet.
- **Public presentation (c0237):** a workflow-oriented README, first-board tutorial and documentation index; actual viewer screenshots, cross-probing animation, social preview and reviewed launch material. Package metadata describes shipped KiCad/Altium features; experimental limits remain visible.
- **Designators placed on the silkscreen** (c0212): `fenolite silk BOARD --designators [--keep] [--refs GLOB] [--sizes LENGTHS] [--angles 0,90] [--clearance LENGTH] [--gap LENGTH] [--measure auto|kicad|estimate] [--drc]` puts the reference of every part beside its courtyard, clear of pad copper, holes, silkscreen, other texts, other parts' courtyards and the board edge, at 0° or 90°, mirrored on the bottom; a row or a column of equal parts shares one place, so their texts line up. The ink of each text is measured with `kicad-cli` (one scratch board, cached per tool version, `fenolite.silk.extents`, `backends.kicad.textink`), or estimated from a glyph table that `tools/measure_glyphs.py` measured (`silk.estimated`). Every written designator is checked again (`silk.check-failed`), `--drc` has KiCad judge the board before and after (`KICAD-VERIFIED`; `H-K-SILK-DRC`, `H-K-SILK-INK`, `H-K-SILK-ESTIMATE`), and a part without room keeps its text (`silk.no-place`). Facts in `docs/formats/kicad/text.md`, user guide `docs/assembly.md`, "Designators". The generated page `commands` of the agent guide may now hold 700 lines, and the skill folder 300 000 bytes. Silkscreen clipping (c0226) and test-point placement (c0229) followed as changes of their own.
- **Silkscreen clipped at the mask openings** (c0226): `fenolite silk BOARD --clip [--margin LENGTH]` cuts every silkscreen line, arc, circle and rectangle or polygon outline of the board and of its footprints where its ink comes near a pad's mask opening (its copper grown by its mask expansion) or a pad's hole, keeping the margin (default: the project's `silk_clearance` rule, else 0) and writing the rest as lines and arcs; filled shapes and texts are not cut (`silk.clip-skipped`). Each piece is checked again (`silk.check-failed`), and `--drc` has KiCad judge the board (`H-K-SILK-CLIP`). A footprint's own outline is cut alike on every instance, so `fenolite library --out` makes one footprint of them; each changed footprint is named (`silk.footprint-changed`) with the `fenolite library` line in `result.library`, and a cut at another part's pads is `silk.cut-by-other`. `--clip` and `--designators` run together, the clip first. Measured first: KiCad's `silk_over_copper` measures to the pad's copper, only for pads with a mask opening, with the limit of a `silk_clearance` rule and not `min_silk_clearance` (`H-K-SILK-MASK`; `docs/formats/kicad/drc.md`, "Silkscreen over pads"). User guide `docs/assembly.md`, "Clipping".
- **Test points placed** (c0229): `fenolite testpoints BOARD --place --side top|bottom [--grid LENGTH] [--reach LENGTH] [--size LENGTH] [--nets GLOB]... [--drc]` adds a probe of `design.test_point` and one straight track for each net the side does not reach, on the nearest grid site near the net's copper that keeps the clearance from other copper, holes and earlier probes, the edge clearance, and stays off courtyards, silkscreen and forbidding rule areas, the probes `--min-pitch` apart. Fenolite's copper check judges the board before it is written (`testpoint.check-failed`); `--drc` has KiCad judge it (`testpoint.drc-new`, `H-K-TP-PLACE`; the library-table type `lib_footprint_issues` aside). The probes are board-only footprints, so a rebuild keeps them and their tracks (`testpoint.board-only`); a net without room is `testpoint.no-site`. `result.place` holds the coverage before and after. `fenolite.silk.board.silkscreen_ink` gives a side's silkscreen as shapes. User guide `docs/assembly.md`, "Placing test points".
- **Group moves, pads paired by net, and zones of a reused block** (c0227): `fenolite place --turn REFS --about X,Y --angle DEGREES` and `--shift REFS --by DX,DY` (the strategy `group`) move a module and its satellites as one rigid piece: each part's angle comes from the move composed with its placement, so bottom parts turn right, and the tracks, vias and zones that join only the group's pads move with it. Every moved pad is measured and the copper check compares the board before and after (`place.group-failed`, error; `place.fill-stale`, info). `--pose-search --within LENGTH` ranks the four turns and a grid of shifts by the demand across the group's border, without writing. `place --strategy reuse` now pairs pads that share a number (three `GND` pads) by the nets of their pads (`result.reuse.parts[].by_net`), and with `--copper` copies a zone of the block whose fill joins paired pads only, with its fill, rebuilding the outline of a zone read without one from its fill (`zone-beyond-block`, `zone-unfilled`, `pad-lost`). Evidence `INFERRED` (`H-G-GROUP-MOVE`, `H-G-REUSE-ZONE`, `H-K-GROUP-DRC`); `docs/placement.md`, "Group moves".
- **Octilinear router** (c0228): `fenolite route BOARD --router octilinear` is a built-in router of short connections, such as gate loops and sense pairs, with no tool and no via. For each open connection (a minimum spanning tree over the pads of a net, pads joined by its copper counting as one) it tries a straight segment, 45° then straight, straight then 45° and the two L shapes on every allowed layer, and keeps the first that keeps the clearance in force from other nets' copper and holes and the edge clearance and makes no acute joint. `--router-option max-length=LENGTH` (20 mm) leaves longer connections open (`route.unrouted`); `--router-option rounds=N` (3) routes the failed connections first again. A differential pair of two pads per net is routed as two tracks at the pair gap (`route.pair-not-routed` otherwise). `route` now gives every router the clearance in force and the copper check of the project's rules in the job's `extra`, and the router removes a connection the check refuses (`route.check-refused`). Router feature `short` in `fenolite capabilities`. Evidence `INFERRED` (`H-G-OCTI-CHECK`, `H-K-OCTI-DRC`; `KICAD-VERIFIED` with `--gate drc`); `docs/routing.md`, "octilinear".
- **Pour shapes and the split of the current** (c0213): `fenolite analyze` gains three opt-in kinds on the raster of the zones in their copper states. `necks` finds the places where a pour narrows below the `min_width_nm` of a `[[neck]]` row (a neck between wider copper, a long thin tip, a sliver), each an `analysis.neck-narrow` error. `loops` measures the area of the closed path through the pads of each `[[loop]]` row, along copper where the pads share a net, judged by `max_area_nm2` (`analysis.loop-area`), and `--loop-baseline FILE` gives its change against another board. `balance` gives the copper at each pad of every two-pad SMD part and their ratio, for tombstoning. `--kinds power --split` solves each power path on the raster and gives every track, fill and via its share of the current, with the resistance checked against the interval of `power`.

- **Practice checks** (c0204): `fenolite analyze` gains six opt-in kinds. `connectivity` gives the groups of pads of each net with every zone in a copper state, `saved_fill`, `simulated` or `outline`: a zone whose fill is missing, or stale (copper where a later keep-out, cut-out, outline or another net's copper forbids it), is poured again on a raster (`--pour-grid`, 0.1 mm by default), and a net that falls apart is `analysis.net-split`. `dead` finds fill islands joined to no pad (`analysis.copper-dead`) and, with `--dead-distance`, copper far from its pads (`analysis.copper-far`). `decoupling` gives the nearest capacitor of each power-input pin along copper and the loop area, judged by a `[[decoupling]]` row (`analysis.decoupling-far`). `bodies` finds copper of a class under the bodies or courtyards of parts named by a `[[body_copper]]` row (`analysis.body-copper`). `relief` measures the spokes of each thermal relief against a `[[current]]` row (`analysis.relief-exceeded`). `coupling --aggressor GLOB --victim GLOB` ranks pairs by `Σ ℓ / (1 + (s/h)²)` with their broadside overlap and each victim's reference coverage; the score ranks pairs and is not a voltage.
- **Initial placement by regions** (c0205): `fenolite place --strategy regions` puts each domain of parts in its own region of the board, packed by the grid: the islands of nets once the `--barrier` parts are removed, or `--group NAME=GLOB[,GLOB...]`, in one band of the outline each (`--split x|y`) or in `--region NAME=X1,Y1,X2,Y2`. Each barrier part (isolator, transformer) is centred across the boundary between the two regions it joins. `result.regions` reports per region the parts placed and not placed and its fill, and the open connections that cross from one region to another; new warnings `place.region-unassigned`, `place.region-barrier` and `place.region-crossing`; evidence `INFERRED` (`H-G-REGION-PLACE`).
- **A Python API for every command** (c0206): `fenolite.api.run(command, *args, cwd=None, seed=None, timestamp=None)` runs any command in the process, through the dispatcher of `fenolite`, and returns a `Reply` with the exit code, the envelope and the error object the command line prints with `--json`, without a subprocess, without printing and without changing the process's working folder; the mutation protocol is unchanged, and a plan id is shared with the command line. Typed calls return records: `read_design`, `read_board` (`Board`), `connectivity` (`ConnectivityReport`), `analyze_current` and `analyze_distances` (`AnalysisReport`). `docs/api.md` lists every public name of `fenolite.api`, frozen for 1.0.
- **Gated and three-way differences** (c0207): `fenolite diff A B --allow track,via` makes each change of another kind a `diff.not-allowed` error (exit 5) naming its path. `fenolite diff BASE OURS --three-way THEIRS --out FILE` plans `THEIRS` with the board items (keyed by uuid) that changed from `BASE` to `OURS` applied, keeping every other byte of `THEIRS`, under the mutation protocol; an item `THEIRS` changed too is `diff.conflict` and nothing is written, and boards of two versions are `diff.unmergeable`. KiCad boards only. The records view of two Altium files counts records that differ only in the spelling of a value (`10mil` and `10.0mil`, `T` and `TRUE`) in `result.resave` and leaves them out of `differences`. `diff` is now a mutating command; without `--three-way` it plans no write.
- **Copper tidy** (c0208): `fenolite tidy BOARD [--prune] [--chamfer] [--leg LENGTH] [--nets GLOB] [--drc]` removes dangling tracks, arcs and vias, again and again until nothing more goes, and turns right-angle joints of two tracks into 45° corners. Each edit stays only when its net keeps its connected groups and the copper check, with the project's rules, finds nothing new; locked and script copper stay. `--drc` runs `kicad-cli` DRC on both boards and writes nothing when a violation type grows (`KICAD-VERIFIED` otherwise; `H-K-TIDY-DRC`). Package `fenolite.tidy`, seven `tidy.*` codes, `docs/copper.md` "Copper tidy". Straightening runs, the style report and the octilinear router stay with c0175.
- **KiCad DRC exclusions written** (c0209): `fenolite check BOARD --exclude CODE:ITEM_A:ITEM_B --reason TEXT --confirm` stores one exclusion in the board's `.kicad_pro`, in both item orders, with the reason as its comment. The finding is named by its code and the items of its `where` (or KiCad uuids); the marker position the key needs is found by probe keys on a copy, and a last `kicad-cli` run confirms the key before the write is planned (`check.exclusion-unmatched`, `-ambiguous`, `-unlocated`, `-present`). `check` judges the two keys as one exception and lists it in `summary.accepted` of `drc.kicad`.
- **Trial edits gated by DRC** (c0209): `fenolite.api.trial(board, edit, gate=False)` applies an edit to a copy, refills the zones (KiCad 10), runs `kicad-cli` DRC on the edited and the unedited copy, and maps each new finding to the ids of the edit's items; with `gate=True` it drops the items that bring new findings until none is left and returns the board to write. A `--gate drc` option of the writing commands stays in c0175.
- **Layout reuse** (c0210): `fenolite place BOARD --from REFERENCE --only U1,C1,C2` (`--strategy reuse`) copies the placement of a block of a reference board (any board a backend reads) onto parts of a KiCad board by a rigid fit over their pads: a quarter turn, a mirror and a translation by least squares, on the pads whose number occurs once in both footprints, first of the anchor (`--anchor`, which stays), then of each part, whose angle and side come from the fit, so a reference read with the Y axis up is copied correctly. `--map TARGET=REFERENCE` copies a second channel, `--tolerance` bounds each pad's distance (`reuse.no-fit` beyond it, `reuse.mirror-suspected` for a part that fits only mirrored). Moved pads are measured against their goals and the copper check runs before and after the moves. `--copper` copies the tracks, arcs and vias joined to the block's pads onto the matching nets, prunes what ends nowhere, drops islands and copies that the copper check flags, and locks the rest; `--drc` compares `kicad-cli` DRC before and after. `result.reuse` holds the transform, the fit per part and the copied and dropped copper. Package `fenolite.reuse`, eight `reuse.*` codes, `docs/placement.md` "Layout reuse"; evidence `INFERRED` (`H-G-REUSE-FIT`, `H-K-REUSE-DRC`). Pads paired by their nets, copied zones and group moves stay with c0175.
- **Region renders** (c0211): `fenolite render BOARD --region X1,Y1,X2,Y2 -o DIR` writes `DIR/region.svg`, one rectangle of the board drawn by Fenolite from the model without `kicad-cli`: pads, tracks, arcs, vias, holes, slots, the board edge and the zone copper in its state (saved fill, simulated pour drawn as its cells, or outline), on a grid labelled in board millimetres (`--grid LENGTH`). `--nets` labels each pad with its net, `--open` draws the open connections as straight lines, and `--cut X1,Y1,X2,Y2` (with `--cut-width`, `--cut-clearance`) draws a barrier cut and its ways per layer from the crossing analysis. Every element carries `class` and `data-*` attributes and exact millimetre coordinates; `result.region` holds the counts, the copper states, the zones and the open connections drawn. New codes `render.region-empty` (warning) and `render.copper-simulated` (info); evidence `INFERRED` (`H-G-REGION-RENDER`). `fenolite.analysis.connectivity.connectivity` takes `pour=` to find the open connections on the copper of the practice checks.
- **Copper tidy straightens, enters T-joints and reports the style** (c0214): `fenolite tidy BOARD --straighten` redraws each run of tracks between fixed points (pads, vias, joints of three) as a shape of at most three segments at 0°, 45° and 90° with fewer weighted turns (45° weighs 1, 90° weighs 3, never acute, free inside the run's own pad), kept only when the net keeps its groups and the copper check finds nothing new; nets of a differential pair or with a length rule stay (`tidy.straighten-skipped`). `--chamfer` also gives a T-joint a 45° entry, and `--zones` cuts the convex right-angle corners of zone outlines, keeping every item the zone touched. With no step option, prune, straighten and chamfer run in that order. `fenolite tidy BOARD --report [--compare REFERENCE]` writes nothing and counts, per net class and layer, the joints at 45°, 90° and acute (`tidy.acute`), the jogs, the widths and the vias (`H-G-TIDY-STYLE`); a run of the steps gives `result.style` before and after. `H-K-TIDY-DRC` now covers the three steps. Tasks 7.2 to 7.5 of c0175; the octilinear router stays there.
- **Library fidelity** (c0223): pads converted from Altium to KiCad get their paste and mask openings by their kind (a surface pad its side's paste and mask, a through-hole pad both masks) and their margins, from a manual expansion or the `All` rule; a pad that a scoped rule may govern is a `pad-margin` loss, but a rule whose scope selects no pad (`IsVia`, `IsTrack`) is left out, so the public documents give none. `Pad.mask_margin` and `Pad.paste_margin` hold them, and the KiCad writers write them as `solder_mask_margin` and `solder_paste_margin`. The embedded model of a body of a top-side part is written beside the derived library (`<name>.3dshapes/`) and placed in its footprint (`FootprintDef.placements`, `ModelPlacement`; `H-K-MODEL-PLACE`, measured with `kicad-cli pcb export vrml`); a model of a bottom-side part or turned about X or Y is a `model` loss. `fenolite library BOARD --out LIB.pretty` writes each footprint of a KiCad board back at its origin, rotation 0 and top side (`library.instances-differ` for a second instance that differs), and `--diff LIBRARY` reports the drift by `pads`, `silkscreen`, `courtyard`, `models` and `fields` (`library.drift`, exit 5) and writes nothing.
- **Generated copper** (c0224): six calls of a design script that the KiCad build resolves after placement into ordinary, locked items named by the call. `design.band(net, spine, width=, max_width=, step=, layers=)` is one zone along a spine of horizontal and vertical runs whose sides widen in 45° steps where the clearance allows; `keepout_ring(part, setback=)` rings each hole of a part; `barrier(netclass, setback=, pieces=False)` keeps a band around a class's copper (two rule areas per piece, a convex envelope); `body_keepout(part, setback=)` covers a part's plan view; `fanout_zone(net, side=)` joins each SMD pad of the net to its zone on another layer with the plane fan-out of `route`; `stitch_pieces(net, pitch=)` ties the overlapping pours of a net. `fenolite.dsl.generated` returns the records; fourteen `kicad.generate.*` codes; `H-K-GEN-DRC`, measured with `kicad-cli` 10.0.7 (no DRC violation type grows; KiCad reports overlapping zones of one priority even on one net, so a band is one zone).
- **DRC gate on route and tidy** (c0225): `fenolite route BOARD --router NAME --gate drc [--kicad-cli PATH]` and `fenolite tidy BOARD --gate drc` make their edit, judge it through `fenolite.api.trial(..., gate=True)` with `kicad-cli` DRC, and write the board without the items that bring new findings: each dropped item gives `gate.dropped` (warning) and is listed with its findings in `result.gate`, a new finding that names no item of the edit gives `gate.unnamed`, and `route` takes its verdict from the kept board. `tidy` keeps or undoes an edit whole, through the new `together` groups of `trial`. Without a `kicad-cli` the command exits 6 before it edits; `tidy --drc --gate drc` is a usage error. A gated write with no finding left is `KICAD-VERIFIED`.

- **The MCP server** (c0195): `fenolite mcp` serves every public command as a tool of the Model Context Protocol on standard input and output, with the standard library only. It speaks the current revision `2026-07-28` and the `initialize` handshake of `2025-11-25`, `2025-06-18` and `2025-03-26`. Each call runs the command line and returns its envelope (the exit code in `_meta` `fenolite/exit`); a writing tool is a dry run until it confirms the plan it returned, and never writes outside `--root`; the guide's pages are resources. `--read-only`, `--tools` and `--timeout` narrow it, `--list` shows what it would serve, and `docs/mcp.md` says how to add it to a client.
- **`fenolite edit`** (c0196): writes a design script, a TOML file or a library table through the write protocol, from a whole new text (`--content-file`) or exact old/new pairs (`--edits-file`, each old text found once). The new text must parse (`ast.parse`, `tomllib`) and is never run; the plan carries a unified diff; the file keeps its line ends.
- **The design and the views of a board in MCP replies** (c0196): `fenolite mcp --allow-edit` serves `fenolite_edit`, which takes the text itself (`content` or `edits`) and writes only through a reviewed plan under `--root`; it is off by default, because editing a design script and building it runs that code as the user. A reply that plans or writes PNG files (`render --png`) carries them as images, from the staged plan of a dry run, at most 4 and 6 MB per reply.
- **Project review status (c0239):** the viewer distinguishes missing, passing, failed and limited checks, keeps evidence/revision scope visible and guides filtering and locating findings with their original hints. `view --report FILE` loads recorded conversion/export results alongside `--check`, including their limitations.
- **Reviewed catalog selection (c0240):** select a symbol and footprint together with isolated offline previews, filter exact pad geometry and explicitly review a complete pin-to-pad map before copying a Python Part fragment.

- **Installed catalog browser (c0234):** `fenolite catalog view --out catalog.html` plans a standalone page of the built-in symbols and footprints, with existing text/kind filters, exact library IDs, evidence, sources and interactive pin/pad inspection. It runs offline without a CAD installation.

- **Fabrication files read back** (c0203): `fenolite export --readback` reads the Gerber, drill and position files with Fenolite's own readers of what `kicad-cli` writes and compares them with the board before anything is written: the outline's contours, every hole and slot (position, diameter, plating), every position row, and a non-empty file for every copper layer. A mismatch is an `export.readback-*` error.
- **Return paths checked** (c0202): `fenolite analyze --kinds returns --net GLOB` measures, per track, the parts of its centre line with no reference copper on a neighbouring layer; a part inside the track is a crossing of a gap (`analysis.return-split`), a part at its end is listed as an edge. `--via-distance` judges each signal via against the nearest reference via (`analysis.return-via`), and `--reference` names the reference nets.

- **File safety for the owner's project** (c0201): `fenolite snapshot` copies a project after complete-set hash checks, with `snapshot.json` (observed hashes, without locking writers); `fenolite publish` puts an edited copy back, replacing only the files whose hash is still the recorded one (`safety.changed-since-receipt` otherwise); every writing command warns `safety.open-session` when KiCad holds a planned file open, and `fenolite doctor --sessions` lists lock files and online-only files; a file a sync client keeps only online stops a read with `FEN-3007`; `fenolite refs --repoint OLD=NEW` lists and rewrites the paths a project's documents hold.

- **Ground ties proved by removing exactly their copper** (c0198): `fenolite analyze --kinds ties --tie REF` or `--tie-item ID[,ID...]` removes a net-tie part's pads, or tracks, vias and zones named by id, and lists the groups of pads left; one group is `analysis.tie-parallel` with the other path item by item, and `--domains PWR=Q*,CTL=U*` names a pad left on the wrong side (`analysis.tie-extra`). `--sense NET,NET` reports sense-net copper that touches another net outside a net-tie group (`analysis.sense-shared`), and `--kinds domains --barrier REF` lists the islands of nets an isolator separates, without reading copper.
- **Barrier crossings counted** (c0199): `fenolite analyze --kinds crossings --cut X1,Y1,X2,Y2` lists, per copper layer, the free ways across the cut (copper grown by its clearance, the board edge, holes and rule areas that forbid tracks) and the tracks of `--width` each fits, then the nets whose open connections or copper cross it; `analysis.crossings-short` warns when the open connections exceed the tracks of all layers.
- **Polygon pours computed for Altium** (c0215, experimental): `pcbdoc.write_pcbdoc(..., pour="computed")` writes the poured copper of every polygon as Fenolite computes it with Altium's own rules from the written document read back: the clearance of the governing rule (`InPolygon` scopes included), the Polygon Connect Style of each pad and via (relief conductors, air gap, angle), pour over same-net objects, `POURINDEX` order, neck removal, island and dead-copper removal, keep-outs, the board edge and cut-outs, and the via pads that a via has on each layer (`backends/altium/pour.py`). The regions are written in `Regions6` and `ShapeBasedRegions6` as a saved pour holds them, after the regions of the board, and the document is read back and self-checked: a polygon whose copper comes nearer to another net than its clearance is refused with every later polygon that depends on it. A polygon the engine cannot evaluate (a hatched or shelved polygon, an arc in its outline, a rule scope or a connect style it does not read, copper text or a polygon cut-out within reach) keeps its outline, with `altium.zone-unpoured` (warning) naming the reason; `altium.zones-poured` (info) counts the poured ones. The evidence is `INFERRED` until a round of kit group K6 on Altium Designer passes (`H-A-POUR-*` in `docs/hypotheses.md`, the inputs in `docs/formats/altium/pcb-pour.md`); KiCad's importer reads the pour as written (`H-A-POUR-KICAD`, 10.0.6). The engine needs the `geo` extra (`pyclipper`).
- **Polygon Connect Style rules** (c0215): a computed document gains one Polygon Connect Style rule per net of its zones (`InNet`), lowered from the zones' connection settings, and one `All` rule (`backends/altium/connectstyle.py`); zones of one net that disagree, and a pad's own `zone_connection`, are `altium.not-lowered`. The Altium import keeps the Polygon Connect Style records of a document on the board (`polygon_connect`), and a computed rewrite writes them back as they were read when every one maps.
- **`--altium-pour computed|outline`** on `fenolite build --target altium`, `fenolite convert --to altium` and `fenolite panel --target altium`, and `pour=` on `lower.write_design`, `AltiumBackend.write`, `lens.altium.build_altium`, `convert_project`, `api.convert` and `panel.altium.flat_files` (c0215). `result.pcb.pour` of a build and `result.panel.pour` of a flattened panel hold the mode, the polygons poured, the refused ones per reason, the evidence and the hypotheses. An explicit `computed` without the `geo` extra exits 6 (`FEN-6001`). The codes `altium.zones-poured`, `altium.zone-unpoured` and `altium.pour-unavailable` are in `fenolite explain`.
- The `clipper` boolean backend gains `intersection`, `difference`, `xor`, `offset` (round, miter or square joins) and `stroke` (c0215).
- **`fenolite.backends.altium.pourcompare`** (c0215): `compare_pours(candidate, reference, *, points=())` compares the pours of two readings of one board polygon by polygon, with `d` the polygon's `ARCRESOLUTION`: M1 the XOR area against `d` times the boundary, M2 each part of the XOR that survives an inward offset by `d` (its centre, area and side), M3 the pieces one to one, M4 the objects each piece touches and each net's connected groups, M5 declared points; `compare_computed` compares the engine's pour of a document with the regions it holds. Nothing is written.
- **A pour census of the public corpus** (c0215): the eight public PCB documents are poured and compared with their saved regions (`tests/corpus/test_altium_pour_census.py`, `docs/evidence/altium-pour.md`): 78 of 81 polygons are refused with a reason, and the three compared meet M1 and differ by M2, so no polygon supports `H-A-POUR-CORPUS` yet; the places are listed.
- **Kit group K6 for computed pours** (c0215): the samples `pour` (eleven probes on two layers) and `pour4` (the inner layers of four), each probe its own polygon and net with declared points, built as `computed/` and `reference/` documents and `probes.json`; steps K6.2 to K6.11 and the checks `repoured`, `pour-match`, `pour-same` and `pour-kept`, with the input rows of the pour settled by their probes. The steps are manual. `fenolite kit build` needs the `geo` extra (exit 6 without), and CI now installs it.
- The Altium import keeps each polygon's `POUROVERSTYLE` (`pour_over`) and each Polygon Connect Style record (`polygon_connect`) in its extension bags, and a write gives them back (c0215).
- **The residue scan over the whole history in CI** (c0220, tasks 1.1 and 1.2): the release workflow runs `tools/residue/scan.py --history` on the public patterns before it builds the distributions, and the nightly runs it as the job `residue-history`; a hit fails the job, so nothing is published. `make residue-history` runs it with the private lists of `private/` when they exist, the maintainer's run before a release; its last line holds counts only. `docs/provenance.md`, "Private residue gate".

### Changed

- **The practice pages point to the commands that now answer their checks** (c0175, task 13.1): the table of `board-practice` gains a column "measured by" (`analyze --kinds loops`, `ties --sense`, `bodies`, `returns`, `crossings`, `decoupling`, `silk --designators`, `testpoints`, `library --diff`), and `board-routing`, `board-review`, `board-floorplan`, `board-power`, `board-emc`, `board-grounds`, `board-insulation`, `board-assembly` and `board-workflow` gain tested lines for `tidy`, `route --gate drc`, `render --region --cut`, `place --from --tolerance` and `--turn`, `analyze --loop-baseline`, `--pour-grid`, `--reference` and `--sense`, `check --exclude`, `export --readback`, `diff --allow` and `--three-way`, `testpoints --place --reach` and `library --diff`.
- **Releases from a tag** (c0221): pushing `vX.Y.Z` runs the release workflow, which refuses a tag whose commit is not on `main` or whose versions, alias pin and changelog section disagree (`tools/release.py check`), builds both packages from the tag, checks their metadata and installs the wheel alone, publishes to PyPI (skipped when PyPI holds the version) and creates the GitHub Release with notes from the changelog, install lines and the files PyPI serves with their SHA-256. A manual run takes a tag and can create it on `main`. It no longer starts on a published GitHub Release. See `docs/releasing.md`.
- **The `mcp` extra is removed** (c0195): the server needs no package, and the official MCP SDK is in the `oracles` extra, where a test runs its client against the server.
- **The agent evaluation can run Claude Code** (c0081, task 4.2): the runner `claude-code` of `tools/agent_eval/runners.toml` is completed against the `--help` of Claude Code 2.1.311 (version flag, isolation with `--bare`, the installed guide given with `--append-system-prompt-file`, a cap of `--max-budget-usd 2`). The run itself is started by the maintainer: `make agent-eval TASK=led-indicator RUNNER=claude-code ARGS=--yes`, with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` set.
- Development: the version is `0.6.0.dev0` after the release of `0.5.0`, so that changes are recorded under Unreleased again; the release of `0.6.0` sets `0.6.0` in the package, its alias and the alias pin.
- **Altium documents are written poured by default** (c0215, the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-26): every command and function that writes a `.PcbDoc` pours its polygons unless asked for `outline`, which gives the files of 0.5.0 byte for byte; without the `geo` extra the default writes the polygons unpoured with one `altium.pour-unavailable` warning. A KiCad-to-Altium conversion reports a poured zone under `zone-fill` with the reason "the pour is computed by Fenolite from the written document (experimental)". The committed Altium samples, the kit's samples and `AltiumBackend.model_roundtrip` keep `outline`, and every test that pins their bytes names it.

### Fixed

- **Historical conversion measurements (c0236):** expose the existing backend mode through `lens.altium.write_model(..., pour=...)` and explicitly reproduce the routed triangle and conversion ledger with `outline`, matching their recorded unpoured inputs after the default changed to computed pours; retain their strict comparisons and the separate computed-pour tests.

- **Portable presentation checks (c0237):** decode the bilingual launch material explicitly as UTF-8 on Windows as well as Linux and macOS, preserving the document content and assertions.

- **Kit provenance declarations (c0236):** register the existing Fenolite-authored c0215 pour and pour4 sample scripts in the data manifest; retain the strict undeclared-file check and the samples' original bytes.

- **Viewer document navigation (c0235):** keep delayed scene loads, errors and polling replies tied to the selected document, including repeated selections and responses arriving out of order.

- **Incomplete write recovery (c0238):** retain original recovery files when rollback itself fails and return non-retryable `FEN-1004` with recovery guidance instead of claiming no files changed.
- **Snapshot consistency (c0232):** revalidate every requested file after each retry, detecting cross-file saves while keeping the bounded retry budget and refusing unstable copies without writing.
- **Viewer review reliability (c0233):** discard stale asynchronous refinements, keep shortcuts out of focused controls and adapt project navigation and tools to narrow review windows.
- **Current documentation (c0236):** describe constrained placement and ERC as shipped, distinguish the published 0.5 feature set from development-only MCP work, preserve dated roadmap decisions, and put complete validation in CI. Reconcile the historical fixture guard with the two hierarchy naming flags deliberately added earlier; no fixture changes.

- **MCP tools of check and diff stay read-only**: since c0209 and c0207 `check` and `diff` can write (`--exclude`, `--three-way --out`); their MCP tools leave those options out, so `--read-only` still serves them and a call needs no plan (`READ_TOOLS` in `fenolite.mcp.tools`). `fenolite mcp --root` is read against the folder `fenolite.api.run` gives.
- **Every `INFERRED` reply names its hypothesis** (c0200): eighteen evidence values were `INFERRED` with no hypothesis; eight hypotheses are registered (`H-G-MODEL-OWN`, `H-G-PLACE-RULES`, `H-G-CATALOG`, `H-G-IMP-TABLE`, `H-G-VIEW-SCENE`, `H-A-LAYOUT-REPLAY`, `H-G-TIES`, `H-G-CROSSINGS`) and each site cites one; a test of the source keeps the v1.0 rule.
- **The router's design file keeps class widths and pairs one-sided clearances** (c0197): a board-wide `track_width` rule with `opt` sets only the classes without a width (the others kept within its limits, with an info), and a one-sided `clearance` rule is written as class pairs instead of raising the class against its own nets. On the plane bench routed with Freerouting, the earlier file left 3 connections open and the new one none. `fenolite route --order` takes several comma-separated globs per tier.
- The DCO check skips `fdafb10f`, the squash merge of pull request 20 made with the GitHub button, which wrote no `Signed-off-by` trailer (`tools/dco_exceptions.txt`; the maintainer's decision of 2026-10-28).

## [0.5.0] - 2026-10-15

### Added

- **Release 0.5.0: v0.5a, board slots and the copper-to-edge check** (c0171): `fenolite convert` converts a project between KiCad and Altium, or writes a KiCad 10 project for KiCad 9, with a report of what is kept, changed or lost and a read-back compared through the public `equivalent` (c0158 to c0162, c0163); `design.slot()` and the edge clearance of the copper check (c0167, c0169); slots in Altium documents, in conversions and as placement and routing obstacles (c0168, c0170); the Draftsman reader and, behind its gate, native Draftsman documents (c0172); board panels with `fenolite panel` (c0173); the viewer `fenolite view` for boards, sheets, libraries, drawings, BOM documents and output jobs, with review tools for Altium projects (c0176 to c0191); the practice pages of the agent guide (c0174); and the native Altium work of c0164 to c0166 and c0092's graduation rule. Every Altium write and both Altium directions of `convert` stay experimental. What each change ships and what waits for the next release is in `docs/release/v0.5.md`.
- **Copper to board edge clearance** (c0169): `fenolite check` (stage `copper.clearance`) and the build's copper guard judge every track, arc, via, pad and zone fill against each ring of the board's edge (the board ring, the cut-outs and the slots, merged as the board file holds them) and report `copper.edge-clearance` (error, or the governing rule's severity) per item and ring when the gap to the edge line is below the edge clearance in force: the last `edge_clearance` rule that selects the item, else the board minimum (KiCad: the project's `min_copper_edge_clearance`, 0.5 mm by default; Altium: the board-wide `BoardOutlineClearance`). The stroke of the edge does not count and a board-wide rule governs below and above the minimum, as KiCad 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 do (`H-K-EDGE-CENTRE`, `H-K-EDGE-RESOLVE`); on a bench of every item kind by the board ring, a round cut-out, a slot and a merged L, the items equal those of KiCad's `copper_edge_clearance` on both majors (`H-K-EDGE-AGREE`; stored fills on 10.0.x). Each finding carries an `EdgeClearanceExplanation` (`ClearanceResolver.edge`, `explain_edge`) and the relation `edge`. Backends give the rings through `BoardFrame.board_edges` (`EdgeRing`), and `DesignRules` gains `min_edge_clearance` and `edge_floor_over_rules`; `result.copper_check` gains `edge_clearance`.
- **Drilled holes in the copper check** (c0169): `copper.hole-in-cutout` (error) for a pad drill, a via drill or a board hole that breaks into a cut-out or a slot or crosses the board edge, and `copper.hole-overlap` (warning) for two drilled holes that overlap, two drilled slots of an L among them. No hole-to-hole value is judged: KiCad's DRC keeps those.
- **Creepage of slots** (c0169): a slot gives exactly the creepage of the same stadium drawn as a cut-out, and two overlapping slots that of their merged ring; a groove width above a slot's width bridges it (`tests/unit/analysis/test_creepage_slots.py`, `docs/analyses.md`).
- **Board slots** (c0167): `design.slot(x, y, *, length, width, rot=0, name=None)` adds a milled, non-plated slot, a stadium of that overall length with a stable id `slot:<name>` (`S1`, `S2`, … by default), and `shape.stadium(x, y, length, width, rot=0)` gives its ring as a path. The model holds it as `OutlineSlot` in `Outline.slots` (id prefix `slt`), its ring from the new `fenolite.core.stadium`; `fenolite.core.trig` now holds the fixed-point cosine table (`geometry.transform` re-exports it, no value changed). A KiCad build writes each slot as a stadium of two lines and two arcs on `Edge.Cuts` (`H-K-SLOT-RING`, KiCad-verified on 9.0.9 and 10.0.6), `board_boundary` and so the creepage of `fenolite analyze` take slots as cut-outs, and `outline.edge_outline` and `outline.slots_of` read a board's edges back with exact arcs and recognise its slots within 1 nm. A drilled slot stays `design.hole(…, length=…)`; `docs/dsl.md` says when to use which. **Release 0.4.x cannot read a model document that carries `slots`.**
- **The union of overlapping cut-outs and slots** (c0167): they are written to KiCad as one ring, every round end kept as an arc (`geometry.rings.merge_rings`, `H-G-RING-UNION`; the L of two slots is seven vertices and three arcs, `H-K-SLOT-UNION`), and the build reports `kicad.outline.merged` (info) naming the items. Before, two overlapping cut-outs stopped the build with `kicad.outline.invalid`, and the model API wrote them as they were, which KiCad calls `invalid_outline` (`H-K-SLOT-OVERLAP`).
- **A `clipper` boolean backend** (c0167): union of integer polygons through `pyclipper` of the `geo` extra (MIT, Clipper under the Boost licence, S-0016), loaded only when it is used; `BACKEND_ORDER` is `("fallback", "clipper")` and `select_backend(operation="union")` picks a backend by operation. `select_backend()` still returns `fallback`.
- `fenolite.core.trig.angle_of`, the microdegree angle of a direction, with integers only (c0167).
- KiCad-to-Altium conversion (c0160): DIFAT sectors for large compound files, plain full-stack pads, connector and free pads, a derived PCB library linked from the project, and source schematic symbols and sheet instances. Edited instances and regenerated presentation are itemized. The 16 light public demo boards convert with zero unexplained differences; heavy cases remain for CI.
- Tolerant imported schematic writes (c0160): Unicode binary properties, preserved Altium parameter references, reported KiCad literal-comment prefixes, and deterministic conflicting-net selection with explicit pin-net losses. Generated libraries disambiguate storage and pin-set collisions.
- **Altium schematic block composition** (c0165): `--altium-layout blocks` or `design.schematic_layout(mode="blocks")` enables functional grouping, automatic flow and pin alignment, quarter-turn passive branches, bounded local wiring and labelled fallback. `design.schematic_block(...)` declares members, titles, anchors and predecessor order. Fixed paper and bounded template occupancy are checked before writes; project-wide native electrical readback refuses changed connectivity or component identities. Builds include `result.schematic_composition`, `.fenolite/schematic-composition.json` and generated-baseline SVG previews in their reviewed transaction. Legacy grid bytes remain unchanged; synced native layouts replay exactly, and changed block intent conflicts even with `--discard-layout`. Evidence is `INFERRED`; native GUI acceptance and font metrics remain unmeasured. See `docs/schematic-composition.md` and the authored before/after example in `examples/schematic_blocks/`.
- **Native Altium schematic layout sync** (c0164): `fenolite sync DESIGN.py --out DIR --to-source --target altium` captures complete edited `.SchDoc` files in `altium-schematic-layout.json` beside the script after checking component IDs, symbol assignments, parameters and connectivity against generated baselines. Altium builds keep those baselines in `.fenolite/altium-generated/` and replay saved sheets byte for byte, retaining placements, rotations, mirrors, wires, text styling and native assets. Changed or removed saved sheets refuse all writes with `build.altium-layout-conflict`, including with `--discard-layout`; changes to an existing sheet need deliberate conflict resolution. New sheets without saved layout use the normal writer. Captures fail closed on changed circuitry or unverified input, and reviewed plans bind the layout inputs. Evidence is `INFERRED`; native Altium GUI acceptance remains unmeasured. See `docs/altium-schematic-layout.md`.
- **A controlled KiCad downgrade through `fenolite convert --to kicad --kicad-version 9`** (c0162): a project saved by KiCad 10 is written for KiCad 9 (board, schematic sheets, the footprint and symbol libraries of the project's own tables, project and rules files), each construct of 10 resolved where it is by the capability resolver `src/fenolite/backends/kicad/data/downgrade.toml`: written in 9's form (`rewrite`), dropped because 9 behaves the same (`same`), or dropped with a reported loss of a drawing or metadata (`presentation`) or of the design (`design`, which needs `--allow-lossy`). The report holds one row per resolver id (kind `downgrade:<id>`, group `downgrade`), and the verification compares the board at level 5 and the root schematic at level 2 under the profile `kicad-downgrade` (`convert.schematic-unverified` when the schematic's netlist needs a `kicad-cli` that is not there). Every resolver row is benched on 9.0.9 and 10.0.6 (`H-K-DOWN-ROWS`, 71 probes `down-row-<bench>`), and the demo projects `CM5_MINIMA_3` and `pic_programmer` load in 9.0.9 with the DRC and ERC types of the source (`H-K-DOWN-DEMOS`, probe `down-demos`); `docs/formats/kicad/versions.md`, "Downgrade", and the column `downgrade` of `docs/formats/kicad/tokens.md`.
- `downgrade=` on `check_target`, `write_board`, `write_footprint`, `write_pretty`, `update_project` and `write_rules`, and the new `sch.retarget_schematic` and `sym.retarget_symbol_library` (c0162); without it every output is unchanged. A board written for 9 by a downgrade gives every zone `(filled_areas_thickness no)`, which 9.0 needs to read a fill as an area (`H-K-ZONE-FAT9`).
- **`fenolite convert SRC --to {kicad,altium} --out DIR`** (c0159): converts a project to another backend or KiCad major, with a report of what is kept, changed or lost, per kind and per reason (`result.report`; `--report-ids` lists the items). Every conversion is read back with the target backend and compared with the source through `fenolite.api.equivalent` under the direction's profile; a difference that no lost item explains is `convert.unexplained` (exit 5, nothing written), and `--no-verify` skips the step with the evidence `UNVERIFIED`. A loss of a `refuse` kind (a footprint, a pad, copper, a net, a net class or a do-not-populate flag) exits 7 (`FEN-7001`) without `--allow-lossy`. `--out` may be an existing folder (`.bak` copies) but never the source's. Two directions: KiCad to Altium (experimental, `INFERRED`) and KiCad to KiCad for the same or a newer major (`KICAD-VERIFIED`, `H-K-CONV-RETARGET`). The library is `fenolite.convert.convert_project` (in memory, no tool) and `fenolite.api.convert`; `docs/conversion.md` describes the kinds, the report and the verification, and `docs/evidence/conversion.md` the 16 KiCad 10.0.6 demo boards converted to Altium with every difference explained (`H-G-CONV-LEDGER`).
- **The result schema `schemas/fenolite.convert.v0.json`** (c0159), and `fenolite capabilities` lists the registered directions under `result.conversions` with their KiCad majors, experimental state and evidence. The codes `convert.lossy`, `convert.changed`, `convert.no-verify` and `convert.unexplained` are in `fenolite explain`.
- **An `altium.not-lowered` info of kind `dnp`** (c0159): an Altium write of a model (`AltiumBackend.write`, `build --target altium`) counts each component marked do-not-populate, since the documents hold no fitted flag outside variants; `dnp` is not a loss of the write (`LOSS_KINDS`), and the PCB document keeps its bytes. `lower.AltiumInputs.lost` keeps every reason per item, and the reason of a pad left out no longer names its number.
- **`fenolite.api.equivalent`**, the public comparison of two designs (c0158): `equivalent(a, b, *, level, tolerances, frame, ignore_refs, profile, against, kicad_cli, timeout)` takes two paths of any side `fenolite equivalent` reads, or two `Design` values, and returns an `EquivalenceResult` (`report`, the sides `a` and `b`, `profile`, `issues`, `evidence`) whose `to_json()` is the command's `result`. A new package `fenolite.api` sits on top of the layering; `import fenolite` does not import it. Usage faults raise `SideUsageError`, a `ValueError`; every error carries the command's `FEN-NNNN` code. `fenolite equivalent` is now a thin caller of it, with the same reply.
- **The result schema `schemas/fenolite.equivalent.v0.json`** (c0158), generated by `tools/gen_schemas.py` from `fenolite.api.schema`, with every key, no other key at any level and the kinds of difference as a closed list; the consistency suite validates the result of every command that has such a file.
- **A KiCad schematic as a side of `equivalent`** (c0158): a root `.kicad_sch` is read with its sheet tree into a circuit and compared at levels 1 and 2 (`netlist_source` `schematic`, `power_symbols`), through Fenolite's own netlist for a schematic that `build` wrote (no tool) and through `kicad-cli sch export netlist` otherwise; the do-not-populate flag comes from the export's `dnp` property (`H-K-EQ-SCHSIDE`, KiCad-verified on 9.0.9 and 10.0.6). `netlist.NetComponent` gains `flags`.
- `fenolite capabilities`: the entry `equivalent` lists its `levels` and the `sides` it reads (c0158); the agent guide's page `checks` gains "Two designs compared".
- The Altium import reads a project's annotation file (`read.annotation`, returned by `load_project` as `annotations`) and takes the designator of a channel component from it before the designator format, after the board (c0083). No public source states the form of that file, so the reader assumes one `<unique-id path>=<designator>` line per entry, reports every other line with `altium.text.unknown-key` (info) and keeps the bytes; the maintainer's Part R, step R4, settles `H-A-IMP-RPT-ANNOT`.
- Agent guide: a `fenolite-design` block whose fence carries `kicad10` is built for KiCad 10 alone (`fenolite.agent.guide.DESIGN_TARGETS`); the page `fabrication` uses it for a stitch whose vias are filled and capped with `protect(filling=True, capping=True)`, which a KiCad 9 board cannot hold (c0112).
- Altium projects can be converted with `fenolite convert --to kicad` for KiCad 9/10
  (c0161, experimental). Outputs include the board, rules, project, derived footprint
  and symbol libraries and regenerated schematics. Mechanical layers map to named
  user layers; closed Keep-Out outlines become rule areas. Changed presentation and
  unsupported items are reported. Seven light public PCB documents have no unexplained
  read-back differences; full independent corpus checks remain for CI.
- `fenolite build --target altium --altium-template FILE.SchDot` applies a native binary sheet template
  to every generated schematic without neutral drawing conversion (c0164). It retains native page
  settings, fonts, special strings, graphic ownership and embedded storage, and copies the supplied
  template unchanged to `templates/sheet.SchDot`. The reviewed plan tracks the template input. ASCII
  output and a script's neutral drawing sheet conflict with this option. Application remains INFERRED
  until checked in Altium Designer.
- v0.3: Altium write kinds leave `experimental` only by a tested rule (own readback, RT-A3 over the
  corpus, every write hypothesis release-verified, a recorded kit run that is not stale); none of the
  nine kinds meets it yet, and `capabilities` lists what each one misses. The acceptance run and its
  result are in `docs/evidence/altium-acceptance.md` (c0092).
- Apply generator-checked Altium graduation decisions per format in capabilities
  and build notices; include missing conditions for all experimental kinds and
  combine evidence only for emitted documents and inputs. Current kinds remain
  experimental; installed packages do not require checkout evidence files.
- Add the authored v0.3 acceptance tree and a preparation mode that builds both
  targets, checks written scope and records JSON results; external import validation
  runs separately in the KiCad CI job.
- Declare own-readback proofs for all nine Altium write kinds and list missing
  graduation conditions in the evidence matrix using current committed kit records.
- Add the pure c0092 write-graduation rule, rejecting missing readback proofs,
  weak or refuted hypotheses, missing RT-A3 and stale or uncommitted kit runs.
  Integration and acceptance remain pending; dependency CI still gates archive.
- Read native indexed Altium annotation records without changing their bytes and
  apply measured one-level Repeat channel mappings; preserve legacy authored
  flat maps and leave unmeasured nested paths unresolved.
- **Slotted board holes** (c0168): `Hole.length` (the overall length of a drilled slot of width `drill`, `None` for a round hole) and `Hole.rotation` (the axis of that length). The canonical writer omits both at their defaults, so a document without slotted holes keeps its bytes. **Release 0.4.x cannot read a model document whose hole carries `length` or `rotation`.**
- **Slot pads in an Altium build** (c0168): a drilled slot, `design.hole(…, length=…)` that is not plated, is written to the PCB document as a free pad with a slotted hole (Multi-Layer, no designator or net, size (length, width), hole shape Slot, the pad turned by the slot's angle), so the copper equals the hole; before, it was reported as not lowered. KiCad 10.0.6's importer reads it as the same oval non-plated hole (`H-A-SLOT-PAD`, probe `altium-slot-pad`).
- **Board cut-out regions behind the kit's gate** (c0168): the cut-outs and milled slots of the outline, each group of overlapping ones merged, can be written as board cut-out regions (`KIND=0` with `ISBOARDCUTOUT=TRUE` on the Keep-Out layer, arcs polygonised within 1 µm; `pcbrecords.cutout_region`). KiCad 10.0.6 imports each as an `Edge.Cuts` cut-out (`H-A-CUTOUT-REGION`, probe `altium-cutout-region`). The form is written only once the maintainer's kit (Part S of `docs/evidence/altium-pcb.md`, cases S3 to S5) has confirmed it in Altium Designer: until then `pcbdoc.CUTOUT_REGIONS` is false and the build reports each cut-out.
- RT-A2 of an Altium build compares board holes (position, drill, plating, length, rotation) and each written cut-out ring (`backends.base.CutoutComparer`).
- **Slots and cut-outs in conversions** (c0170): `fenolite convert --to altium` takes the edges of a KiCad board with `edge_outline`: the board ring is written as before and every inner ring (cut-out or slot) follows the cut-out region lowering of c0168, written behind its gate or lost with the gate's reason. `fenolite convert --to kicad` merges overlapping cut-out rings into one (the `geo` extra); an overlap that cannot be merged is not written and is a loss that needs `--allow-lossy` (exit 7), since KiCad would call the outline malformed.
- **The report kind `cutout`** (c0170): every inner edge ring of a converted board is counted once under `cutout` (group `board`), kept, changed (`merged`, `arcs polygonised`) or lost; the kind `outline` keeps only the losses of the board ring.
- **Edge rings verified after a conversion** (c0170): the rings of the source's edge and of the written board read back are compared within 1 µm plus the region chord; a ring that differs and that the report does not explain is a `convert.unexplained` error of kind `edge-ring` (exit 5).
- **Slotted drills to Altium** (c0170): a footprint pad of one shape with an oval drill is written to the PCB library and document with hole shape Slot, its slot length and its slot rotation relative to the pad, instead of refusing the footprint.
- **Generated hole footprints from Altium** (c0170): `fenolite convert --to kicad` gives each board hole of an Altium document the footprint that `design.hole` generates (`Fenolite_Holes:NPTH_…`), its library written as `lib/Fenolite_Holes.pretty`; the builder moved to `fenolite.model.holes` and `fenolite.dsl.holes` re-exports it with the same bytes. A plated hole is counted changed.
- **Slots as placement and routing obstacles** (c0170): the constrained placer's hard checks, its preview and the body-volume analysis take the slots of the outline as they take cut-outs, and a Specctra design file holds each slot as a `keepout` polygon, which Freerouting routes around (`H-G-DSN-SLOT`).
- **Board panels: the panel script and model** (c0173): a script of its own binds `panel = Panel(name, board=…)` (`fenolite.dsl.Panel`) over a built `.kicad_pcb` or `.PcbDoc`: `grid(rows, cols, …)` or `fit(width, height, …, rot="auto")`, `rails()`, `separate("vscore" | "tabs", between="columns" | "rows" | "rails")`, `tabs()` with mouse bites, `vscore()`, `fiducial()`/`fiducials()` and `tooling_hole()`/`tooling_holes()`, in integer nanometres and quarter turns; `model.panel.Panel` is its canonical document (schema `schemas/fenolite.model.v0/panel.json`), with ids derived from the copy keys. Boards whose outline is one rectangle only (`panel.board-outline` otherwise). See `docs/panels.md`.
- **`fenolite panel PANEL.py --out DIR --target {9,10,altium}`** (c0173): a writing command (`--dry-run`, `--confirm --plan`, `--discard-layout`) that lays the panel out and checks it before anything is planned: `panel.vscore-copper`, `panel.vscore-shape`, `panel.vscore-spacing`, `panel.vscore-thickness`, `panel.tab-copper`, `panel.gap`, `panel.tab-count`, `panel.tab-spacing`, `panel.fiducial-place`, `panel.tooling-place`, `panel.size` and the structural errors, each limit a default of `panel.limits.DEFAULTS` citing a public fabricator page (`H-G-PANEL-LIMITS`) and replaceable with `Panel(limits={...})`; an error plans no write (exit 5).
- **KiCad panel board** (c0173): `--target 9` or `10` writes `<name>.kicad_pcb` with every copy moved exactly and its nets renamed `B<k>/<name>` (designators kept), the panel's outline with the routed gaps as inner rings, V-score lines on a user layer named `V-Score`, mouse bites as generated `Fenolite_Panel:MouseBites_…` footprints, c0118's fiducial and tooling-hole footprints, its project and `<name>.panel.json`. `KICAD-VERIFIED (9.0.x, 10.0.x)`: it loads, its DRC gives each copy the source's findings only, and its holes reach the NPTH file (`H-K-PANEL-LOAD`, `-NETS`, `-DRILL`).
- **Flattened Altium panel** (c0173): `--target altium` writes `<name>.PcbDoc` (the copies, the panel's rectangle as board shape, the routed gaps on a mechanical layer named `Route Tool Path`, the score lines on one named `V-Cut`, free non-plated hole pads and free fiducial pads) and a `<name>.PrjPcb` holding it alone, with one `altium.panel-flattened` info. KiCad 10.0.6's importer finds every item at the KiCad panel's places within 10 nm (`ORACLE-VERIFIED(kicad-cli)`, `H-A-PANEL-FLAT`).
- **Altium's Embedded Board Array behind its gate** (c0173): `pcbdoc.EMBEDDED_ARRAYS` is false, so `--altium-panel embedded` exits 7 until the maintainer's kit (Part Q of `docs/evidence/altium-pcb.md`, cases Q7 to Q9) confirms a written array in Altium Designer; nothing about the array is claimed before (`H-A-PANEL-RECORD`, `H-A-PANEL-LAYERKIND` `UNKNOWN`, `H-A-PANEL-KIT` `INFERRED`). Part Q phase A is built for the maintainer.
